## Scope and evidence

P implements the revised P.1-P.6 requirements: explainable hierarchical budgets, four deterministic safe routine actions, Today freshness/failure visibility, honest host/process operation and a bounded explicit local backup/restore path. It stops at P; Q operating views, R/S extensions, T rollout, external benchmarks and live-project pilots are not prerequisites or authorized work.

The product remains a single-user local-first modular monolith with file-first state. `dashboard/src/server/serve.ts` starts API and the existing worker in one process; `runner/task-orchestrator.ts` owns ticks/leases. `runner/process-manager.ts` holds project-command history in memory. A durable work session does not prove its linked process survives a restart.

Existing constraints are normative: `lib/context/types.ts`, `registry/context-policy.yml`, `lib/state/orchestration.ts`, `lib/workflows/policy.ts`, `registry/workflow-policy.yml`, `runner/run-task.ts`, context admission v1/v2, private workflow ownership and exact scope/candidate receipts. P cannot replace these with ledger metadata or prompt instructions. Canonical issue transitions remain v1; no new edge or terminal-state rewrite is proposed.

Three implementation slices require sequential context loads: budget/runtime, routine/dashboard, then state continuity. Read each routed skill/document completely; the union plus these artifacts exceeds 500 physical lines. Do not raise route limits, refresh snapshots, edit skill versions or invent historical approvals.

## Approved contracts and defaults

| Contract | Ownership and persistence |
| --- | --- |
| BudgetPolicy v1 | Trusted versioned `registry/budget-policy.yml`; exact source fingerprint, scope/dimensions, warning and hard caps, optional UTC period. No caller/agent authority. |
| BudgetLedger v1 | One bounded aggregate at `state/budgets/budgets.json`: reservations, canonical run charges, scope/period projections and uncertainty. All relevant dimensions update in one critical section. |
| BudgetAdmission v1 | Application-created immutable task-bound subdocument, optional only for historical reads, required for new admission/execution. Strict independent schema; context admission v1/v2 keep their meanings. |
| Routine/Occurrence v1 | `state/routines/routines.json`; exact routine revision, schedule slot, intent, private ownership, receipt and finite catch-up. No generic job/plugin framework. |
| BackupManifest v1 | Explicit local snapshot directory, closed catalog and raw-file presence/version/size/SHA-256, creation time, reference assessment and exclusions. Not a signed trust statement. |
| Maintenance/recovery controls v1 | Durable hold, journal, revoked authority IDs and accounting high-water outside the replaceable snapshot payload. A hold is not a product lifecycle status. |

Defaults approved with the design, not fabricated clarification answers:

- No new aggregate resource numbers are guessed from business goals. Unconfigured dimensions are explicitly `unconfigured`, never zero/infinite JSON values; finite existing leaf/workflow caps still apply. Trusted configured hard caps may only restrict them.
- Warnings default to 80% of a configured hard cap; they never permit execution. Period is `none` unless explicitly `utc-day` or `utc-month`. Concurrency includes reservations across periods; rollover does not release unresolved holds.
- Monetary reporting remains unavailable without an exact versioned pricing source and matching measured model/usage. P ships no price table, estimates or billing integration; a mandatory currency cap is rejected rather than ignored.
- Routines are disabled initially, manual `schedule: null` or the grammar `every:<integer>m`, 15..10080 minutes, UTC anchored to the stored schedule revision. Default `skip` concurrency and `skip-missed`; explicit `coalesce`/`run-once` allow at most one pending catch-up, never a backlog replay.
- One routine at a time, at most 25 projects per occurrence, one attempt and 60000 ms total runtime; existing worker/project/read constraints can lower these. Provider-disabled or oversized scopes produce visible unavailable/partial evidence, not silent fresh success. Dependencies/provider reads have bounded asynchronous deadlines within this total.
- New aggregates/control journals are capped at 16 MiB and 10000 records per collection. Snapshot payload is capped at 2048 files/256 MiB, retaining tighter existing file/schema limits. Exceeding a limit is a visible refusal, not eviction or compaction that loses budget/authority facts.
- Backup retention defaults to at most ten snapshots and 1 GiB in the selected dedicated boundary. Reaching either cap refuses creation. No automatic deletion; the preimage required by a pending restore is retained regardless and cannot be evicted to fit a new operation.

## Budget resolution and accounting

Applicable constraints are company, selected objective and its existing validated ancestors, project, issue where present, role and execution, plus current worker/workflow/correction policy. The most restrictive configured remaining hard cap wins for each dimension: runtime, attempts, cumulative tokens, concurrent reads/writes, review attempts/runtime/tokens and automated corrections. Review and correction are independently inspectable labels of canonical charges, not extra copies added to the total.

Attribution is server-owned and frozen: company `surtec`, `issue.objective_id` otherwise the project's primary linked objective, otherwise no objective; maximum ancestry depth remains the current eight. Project/objective links form a graph, not an invented single-parent project tree. Changing links cannot transfer old consumption or charge every secondary link. Personal work without an objective remains supported.

The read model shows cap, consumed, reserved, remaining or unavailable, warning, period, policy IDs/revisions/fingerprints, review/correction breakdown and machine-readable denial. Remaining uses consumed plus outstanding reservations. Period buckets are fixed at admission; active/unknown reservations survive rollover. Unknown interrupted usage is never treated as zero or refunded by restart.

Admission sequence:

1. Preview resolves trusted policy/attribution and existing scope/context without reserving or executing. Balances/receipt histories are not instruction sources or immutable context fingerprints.
2. On exact human confirmation or already-approved bounded workflow stage authority, recompute current availability and atomically reserve every applicable dimension. Reserve the admitted stage/task cap, not both a whole workflow grant and its child stage. Failure of one dimension creates no partial reservation.
3. Persist a deterministic task admission intent and its BudgetAdmission v1 binding. The binding identifies task/project/role/agent, scope and policy versions, period buckets, manifest/cap fingerprint and reservation; for tasks whose context admission is v2, BudgetAdmission v1 additionally binds exact workflow/stage/generation/grant. No BudgetAdmission v2 is proposed. It is not a live controller proof.
4. The existing worker creates run IDs at claim. Before every model/worktree/verification effect, require committed matching intent/reservation, unchanged restrictive policy, private controller/lease ownership and all current scope/context/consent gates. Revalidation may exclude only this task's exact reservation; it cannot increase the admitted cap or lend it to another task.
5. Account attempts/active runtime and normalized cumulative usage once per exact task/run. Duplicate, decreasing or repeated terminal notifications are idempotent monotonic deltas. Task and workflow counters describe the same underlying usage; do not sum them again.
6. Settle only proven usage and unused reservation. Cross-store persistence gaps, missing final receipts, lease loss and ambiguous interruption keep a conservative hold/unknown classification and block affected admission pending evidence, never automatic refund or replay.

Use the current short synchronous locking discipline, but a dedicated budget lock with token/inode ownership and no age stealing, modeled on the workflow store. No await, network/Git effect or nested scheduler/task/issue lock inside its callback. Cross-store operations use durable intents and revision fences; do not claim a multi-file transaction. Test admission/claim/settle crash points and the final unit of capacity contested by two requesters/workers.

Preserve existing per-attempt leaf runtime and cumulative task tokens, while configured parent/workflow runtime is cumulative across runs. Admission reserves the first prospective attempt; each retry needs a fresh exact owned atomic top-up under its unchanged admitted task cap, remaining parent limits and normal retry/controller rules. A held task token allowance survives retry and decreases only by proven consumption; never reserve it twice.

| Dimension | Reservation and consumption |
| --- | --- |
| Attempts/review attempts | Hold one prospective attempt before its claim, charge once at claim, reserve the next only if permitted. The immutable task attempt ceiling is not consumption. |
| Runtime/review runtime | Hold one admitted per-attempt cap, debit proven active run time; a retry reserves another bounded run slice. Cumulative parent/workflow limits reduce the next slice. No implicit cumulative leaf cap is introduced when parent runtime is unconfigured. |
| Task tokens/review tokens | Hold the permitted remaining cumulative token allowance; move monotonic proven deltas from reserved to consumed, keep uncertainty held. Review is a label, not duplicate total usage. |
| Concurrent reads/writes | Hold one applicable execution slot, not the entire configured concurrency cap; release only after proven terminal ownership/effect completion. Pending/unknown ownership is explicit. |
| Corrections | Hold the one existing opted-in lineage allowance before correction admission, consume once; review stages and root accounting remain separately bounded. |

The runtime adapter must preserve absent/incomplete/invalid usage as unknown, including after a previously valid intermediate notification; retain proven monotonic charges without declaring a missing final total complete. Explicit complete zero is distinct from a missing total. Tests cover missing/null/negative/non-finite/fractional, decreasing, duplicate and incomplete terminal receipts. Include a two-attempt regression whose cumulative runtime exceeds the per-attempt cap but fits configured parent caps (or no parent runtime cap).

### T006 exact product-runtime target supplement

In addition to the portable task's listed application/runtime targets, T006 explicitly includes `runner/codex-app-server.ts` and `runner/codex-app-server.test.ts`. The current `usageFrom` substitutes zero for absent counters, which loses the information the ledger requires. This exact two-file product-specific supplement belongs here rather than in portable Harness rules; approval of T006 includes these two real source/test paths, not other provider files, generated protocol, model settings, request fields or permissions. Structural task validation does not attest this product appendix or authorize its implementation.

Runtime/attempt/token hard stops apply before admission and cancellation at observed exhaustion. A provider can report tokens after producing them; retain actual excess and deny successors rather than promise an exact billing/token ceiling unsupported by its protocol. Policy tightening never expands an old cap; material policy/attribution changes require fresh preview/authorization.

Historical unversioned/v1/v2 envelopes remain readable with unchanged bytes and no invented admission. A present malformed/null/future BudgetAdmission fails before the legacy validator return. After P's explicit quiescent cutover, historical queued/retry work without a binding is not executable; require a fresh normal admission. Cutover is unavailable until T015-T017 demonstrate the complete cooperative boundary and current technical readiness; T005/T006 earlier slices are scaffolding/fixtures with new P effects blocked, not a production activation. The cutover is an explicit operator-confirmed procedure, never enabled by caller/task metadata or a permissive environment switch. Existing v2 retry/controller denials remain. No bulk backfill, implicit migration, old-grant upgrade or automatic authority repair is proposed.

Exhaustion preserves exact workflow/stage/task/run/candidate and policy evidence. It uses existing issue edges: `authorized -> needs-decision` / `authorization-stale`, `working -> needs-decision` / `decision-required`, or `checking -> needs-decision` / `verification-needs-decision`, as applicable. Terminal/human-review states are not rewritten. Reviewer/QA exhaustion or correction limits cannot bypass a required review, create an extra correction or activate a Reviewer/Engineer loop. Preserve current root/correction ceilings and opt-in defaults.

## Safe routines

The four actions are code allowlisted: `refresh-evidence`, `evaluate-signals`, `propose-issues`, `generate-local-report`. Scope is company/objective/project with validated existing IDs; there is no free-form command, URL, path, model prompt, adapter or executable metadata. Mutating the routine definition requires protected local HTTP/session checks and expected revision; source configuration must be revalidated before each occurrence.

Reuse the existing worker tick with a finite routine hook, not a second timer/service/scheduler. A unique occurrence key binds routine ID, revision and UTC slot; durable admission precedes effects, private ownership precedes execution, and completed slots never run twice. `coalesce` retains at most one pending slot. Restart may perform at most the explicitly configured catch-up; ambiguous occurrence ownership needs review. Disabling or cancellation prevents new effects, without deleting accounting or history. Dispatch one tracked background occurrence after normal task claims without awaiting its completion inside `runOnce` or keeping `ticking` true; own its cancellation/completion in the existing worker lifecycle. Tests prove both heartbeat and new task claims/cancellation progress during a delayed fake adapter.

The ledger supports two explicit subject kinds: task admission/run and routine occurrence/revision/slot. Routines use internal occurrence-owned reservation proofs, never fabricated TaskEnvelopes, agent roles or context admission v2. Apply company/selected objective ancestry/project scope and trusted routine policy as relevant; no issue/agent role is invented. For a multi-project refresh, freeze the bounded selected project IDs and conservatively intersect their applicable read/runtime limits for the whole occurrence. Scope projections overlap and are not summed; do not invent per-project elapsed time or cost. Routine consumption is one known deterministic attempt, measured runtime and one read slot, with exactly zero model calls/tokens and no write execution slot. Unknown resource evidence still blocks the relevant measured dimension.

Actions are deterministic and make no model calls. They may read trusted Git/CI/deployment/dependency/health facts, evaluate existing pure signal rules, flag stale company/objective/project/issue/session/Harness facts, write bounded local receipts/reports or propose a local issue for human review. An occurrence-specific proposal key prevents duplicate issues across restart; it never authorizes the proposal, changes an existing issue/objective, starts workspace-write work or publishes anything.

Read-only does not mean offline: external provider reads require existing explicit trusted integration configuration and URL/argument/environment protections. Missing provider consent/configuration is unavailable evidence, not implicit enablement. Tests use fake providers, a controlled clock and synthetic repositories with network disabled.

Do not call migration-bearing portfolio list reads from routines. Add a validated no-migration reader that preserves v1/v2 bytes or returns a visible migration requirement. Do not reuse list helpers that skip invalid tasks/notes as authoritative facts. Invalid required state blocks that occurrence without making the whole platform unavailable.

Current Git/provider/dependency helpers can block synchronously for 20..60 seconds against a 30-second lease. Routine-specific refresh uses bounded asynchronous subprocess/network paths with existing fixed arguments, reduced environment and trust rules; no install/update command or arbitrary shell. This is not a wholesale endpoint rewrite. A delayed fake adapter must prove concurrent worker heartbeat and cancellation remain effective. Shared TTL/coalescing prevents multiplication of refresh calls per GET/tick.

Receipts contain action/scope/policy/slot IDs, start/end time, freshness/counts, hashes, budget outcome and sanitized failure/next action. They exclude private notes, instructions, raw provider payloads and credentials. The report destination is the code-owned `reports/routines` directory under the operator's explicitly selected canonical local state root; only a safe occurrence ID determines its filename. There is no caller-defined destination, path field in Routine or report-export endpoint. Validate privacy/containment before writing, and report unavailable if that boundary is unsafe. Today presents last/next run, stale/unavailable/partial results and exact failures separately from API/worker health; no optional routine outage is a global outage.

## Process ownership, stop and reconnect

The supported operator path is the existing `pnpm dev` / API worker process on one available machine, with the built dashboard path documented separately. Browser close disconnects a client only. Stopping the API process/terminal or powering off the host stops ticks and model ownership; no offline work or persistent terminal backend is promised. Reload/reconnect requires a current local session, durable task/issue/workflow reads and honest unavailable in-memory process history.

Graceful shutdown prevents new claims/routines, requests cancellation of owned work, settles only known accounting, stops only project commands owned by its current process manager, then closes listeners. Unknown processes/locks are not killed or stolen by age. Abrupt loss goes through existing conservative reconciliation; persistent work sessions and stored leases alone do not prove a live process.

Today and issue/workflow detail show the exact pending decision, current source evidence and next safe action after reopening. Missing process/Git/worktree/candidate/log references are `unavailable` or review-required read-model evidence, not invented domain statuses, reconstructed executions or successful checks. Preserve old receipts as history without calling them currently usable.

## Quiescence and snapshot catalog

Use an explicit offline CLI operation over the existing state command, not a backup server/DB or automatic browser restore. Source, backup boundary and restore target are explicit canonical local directories with no symlink/junction ancestors or hard-linked payload files. Reject workspace/home/drive roots, root aliases, unsafe overlaps, traversal and unsupported remote destinations. Any shell examples operate on exact contained targets, never recursive broad cleanup.

A cooperative maintenance barrier participates in API/worker/CLI writes and effects, including event appends, every state store and notes, implicit migrations, project-command starts and task/workflow admission/claim. Ownership spans each complete read-modify-write, not only its final rename: a read begun before restore cannot later overwrite restored state after release. Fence before any mkdir/append/staging/migration or external effect. Taking a lock only the backup tool recognizes is insufficient. Acquire private exclusive maintenance ownership only after no live task/routine/project-command owner, in-progress mutation or unknown lock remains. The offline CLI refuses an active/unknown owner; it does not automatically stop another process. Do not use stealable 30-second locks as a long snapshot fence.

Validate exact raw bytes before copying; readers which omit corrupt files, migrate on GET or fabricate empty documents cannot certify a snapshot. A closed revisioned catalog covers:

- `tasks/*.json`, `events/*.jsonl`, `projects/*.json`, `notes/*.json` with safe existing identifiers; legacy `Note[]` gets an explicit strict bounded validator, not filtered `listNotes` output.
- `organization/organization.json`, `issues/issues.json`, `portfolio/projects.json` (explicit supported v1/v2, no migration), `portfolio/clients.json`, `sessions/work-sessions.json`, `signals/human-state.json`, `harness/profiles.json`, `workflows/workflows.json`.
- P's budget and routine documents when present, including uncertainty/slot history. Optional absence is explicit; corrupt/future/unsafe source is a refusal, not silently omitted data.

The manifest includes snapshot ID, catalog revision, canonical source identity, creation time, each exact file's presence/version/byte count/SHA-256, schema/config/source revision references, known exclusions and assessment of project Git/worktree/candidate references. Validate cross-record IDs/revisions/bindings; missing event sequences/artifacts remain explicit incomplete evidence. Supported legacy formats are named, not rewritten with a fake `schema_version`.

Exclude locks, auth/session secrets, `.env`, credentials, raw provider payloads, derived reconciliation, old backups/staging, source repositories/worktrees and raw execution logs/diffs/reports. Trusted registry/code/schema are references and hashes only; restore cannot overwrite policy with data. Local notes may be confidential: require an explicitly chosen private directory, owner-only modes where supported and documented Windows ACL verification. Do not claim encryption, complete secret discovery or universal ACL enforcement. Hashes detect byte changes, not authenticity against a malicious same-user writer.

## Restore, rollback and authority quarantine

Preview validates snapshot identity/version/bounds/catalog/path/reference bindings and current target inventory without mutation, migration, provider refresh or runtime calls. It produces an exact create/replace/remove-known-file set, current precondition hashes, optional-absence tombstones, unavailable references and a canonical plan fingerprint. Unknown target files are untouched. A tombstone can remove only an explicitly displayed, confirmed known file whose current hash still matches. Confirmation must name the target and exact plan fingerprint; no general `--force`/blanket confirmation.

Before the first payload mutation, under proven quiescence: persist and verify the target byte preimage, a durable operation journal and root restore hold outside the replacement set; also union prior/current/snapshot proven accounting floors and revoked task/run/grant/intent/resume/candidate-publication IDs in a bounded recovery guard. Include routine ID/revision, occurrence/intent/reservation identity and completed/uncertain slot/proposal-key high-water: a snapshot cannot revive a later-disabled routine or replay a local proposal. Missing/corrupt/future required guard/journal is fail-closed. Never restore older ledger bytes into a refund or make unknown consumption/correction allowance known-zero. Cold restores without later accounting facts remain visibly uncertain.

Use same-volume staging, file fsync and individual atomic renames with exact journal checkpoints. Multi-file restore is not transactional; a persistent hold prevents startup reconciliation, ordinary mutations, routine proposals and execution while a replacement/journal is incomplete. Read routes with migrations/refresh or filesystem side effects are also denied; only explicitly side-effect-free continuity/inventory inspection is available. Test every interruption boundary, not just a final successful round-trip.

Rollback uses preserved preimage only if current outputs still match this journal's recorded writes. Conflict retains current bytes, journal, preimage and hold for inspection; never overwrite unrelated newer user changes. Revoked identities and high-water remain conservative even after rollback. No automatic deletion of snapshots, journals, backups or user state; cleanup/retention instructions require exact independently confirmed ownership.

Releasing the temporary hold is a separate explicit CLI confirmation of root/inventory/journal/guard fingerprint, requiring completed restore or verified rollback and no live/unknown owner. It only changes operational availability: no migration, reconciliation, lifecycle rewrite, new preview, refund, grant renewal or automatic resume. Permanent revocations/high-water survive release and restart.

Restored history never authorizes model/worktree/verification/publication effects, old workflow admission recovery, successors, correction, task retry or thread resume. Restored routine definitions are inspectable but cannot run, catch up or propose until a fresh protected human configuration confirmation creates a current revision under live policy and preserved slot/proposal history. New IDs/revisions do not erase prior uncertainty or duplicate suppression; neither hold release nor rollback re-enables routines. These fences apply before effects and late callbacks, not merely in HTTP/UI. Reuse current read-only reconciliation/ownership assessment behind the guard; do not recreate missing repositories/candidates or trust a stored lease. Operator actions use existing safe human inspection/stop/cancel/rework/resume paths where those already apply, otherwise the record remains blocked for review. Fresh work requires a new current preview and normal exact human authorization; it cannot wash old uncertain scope/lineage accounting through new IDs. There is no arbitrary accounting forgiveness/reset tool in P.

A restored lease whose timestamp is still in the future is historical/revoked, not proof of a live process or occupied live execution slot. Project capacity separately from conservative budget holds/unknown charges; never clear a resource hold merely to improve the view, and never alter lifecycle/lease timestamps to conceal quarantine. Independent fresh work is allowed only when its actual applicable caps, ownership and current authorization permit it.

## Acceptance and delivery

Four catalog checks are proposed: full fake-protocol/state/UI unit/integration tests, schema compilation, typecheck and build. They are not executed as P acceptance during drafting. After exact human approval and implementation, use the guarded 900-second per-command maximum/default unchanged. If fresh runtime exceeds it, report and obtain review; do not silently raise limits or weaken assertions.

Mandatory complementary acceptance (isolated fixtures, no paid runtime or normal state):

1. Contract/hierarchy/period/warning matrices and historical byte preservation; reject forged/future/missing admission and mandatory currency caps without pricing.
2. Contested last capacity, no partial holds, exact self-reservation, duplicate/decreasing usage, retry, policy drift and crashes around reserve/persist/claim/settle. Zero unauthorized model/worktree/provider/publication effects on denials.
3. Independent Reviewer/QA/correction exhaustion reaches the exact existing `needs-decision` edge; no extra role, correction or implicit success. Retain all O regression coverage.
4. Allowlist/injection, pure legacy reads, disabled/catch-up/coalesce/duplicate/restart/cancel routine cases; fake slow provider does not starve task heartbeat or broaden capabilities. Failure/partial freshness visible in Today.
5. Synthetic real protected HTTP plus local browser: inspect budgets/provenance, denied and permitted fake execution, configure/disable/run routine, reload/API restart/session rotation, locate pending decision and unavailable process history. Extend the existing isolated fixture, not a user's running project.
6. Backup raw-byte round-trip/absence, confidential exclusions, invalid/future/link/traversal/bounds and concurrent-owner refusal. Restore exact-set/stale-confirmation, missing Git/candidate, every staged interruption/rollback conflict, restart hold, high-water/no-authority-resurrection and explicit release. Include later-disabled routines, completed/uncertain slots and proposal keys across restore/release/rollback/reconfiguration; no duplicate effect or implicit re-enable. Fixture cleanup verifies owned boundaries even after deliberate faults.

Run `pnpm ci:check`, `pnpm audit:check`, two fresh `pnpm smoke:local:playwright` campaigns, existing workflow browser opt-ins (`SURTEC_WORKFLOW_BROWSER_SMOKE=1` and `SURTEC_WORKFLOW_UI_BROWSER_SMOKE=1`) and the focused P continuity faults separately; never count optional skipped browser steps as passed. Use the existing test files/fixture for their exact commands, restore temporary environment flags in finally blocks and keep current guarded catalog unchanged.

Each TXXX checkpoint records target diff, focused commands/observed outcomes, compatibility/security impact and remaining steps. The final runbook records exact counts, schema versions, synthetic effect counters, cleanup flags, platform limits and sanitized failure evidence. No manual acceptance is delegated when repeatable; only subjective UI preference, real-machine availability/private-boundary choice and future explicitly authorized live work remain operator choices. Stop after P acceptance for owner validation; no push/merge/deploy/paid smoke is inferred.

## T001 implementation boundary - 2026-10-05

The six exact task targets now define BudgetPolicy, BudgetLedger and BudgetAdmission v1 with pure strict validators and 200 synthetic tests. Independent context v1/v2 remains unchanged, inspected directly by the tests; this slice does not claim a new legacy migration or browser round-trip. Frozen attribution, policy/source fingerprints and original UTC buckets are data bindings, not controller/lease/approval proofs. One canonical charge carries overlapping review/correction labels, preserves unknown versus proven zero, permits observed excess without clipping and rejects contradictory settlement. Occurrences have separate subjects, known-zero model resources and no invented role/issue/task authority. Equivalent UTC slots are compared by epoch without rewriting bytes.

Contracts enforce 16 MiB documents, 10000 records per collection, at most 25 projects/eight objective IDs/64 policy bindings, 160-character safe IDs and nesting depth 32. Oversized/unsafe/inherited/hooked/future/contradictory data fails rather than being trimmed, coerced, migrated or replaced with empty state. ## T002 implementation boundary - 2026-10-05

The three approved targets add a pure policy resolver and a neutral trusted registry. Applicable company/objective ancestry/project/issue/role/execution limits intersect finite application-owned existing caps and separately resolved availability; no parent number is invented. Attribution is copied/frozen and historical consumption never follows later link changes. An existing issue keeps its explicit objective anchor after unlink; this attribution calculation does not bypass the unchanged context admission gate requiring a currently linked objective.

Canonical validated ledger projections retain outstanding holds and unknown resource evidence across UTC rollover, separate review labels without duplicating total charges, and count a correction allowance once per exact retained candidate lineage, including replacement workflows. Missing lineage identity makes correction accounting unavailable rather than inventing an allowance. Safe integer overflow refuses the preview. The resolver takes trusted application-read snapshots; its data validation and `can_admit` preview are not ownership, consent, allocation or execution authority. It has no self-reservation exclusion or persistence effects. ## Proposed T015 performance supplement - 2026-10-13 local

Status: approved for the exact six code/test targets and two existing evidence documents by the owner in the conversation on 2026-10-13 local, after reviewing this proposal. The earlier approval covered preparation only; the subsequent explicit approval covers implementation starting with isolated measurements. The original spec/design approval metadata, T001-T014 acceptance and T015 pending status remain unchanged. This is a corrective supplement to the open T015, not a new entity, roadmap phase, retrospective approval or timeout waiver. No product, test or profiler implementation was changed or benchmarked while drafting it; fresh implementation results are recorded separately below.

The unchanged proposal checkout is `codex/phase-p-budgets-routines-continuity` at parent `53ed01fd23bf62f1f08381b48f74b70595ef33f5`, with an empty index. Tool-specific checkout identity belongs in this product appendix, not the portable task core.

### Measured baseline and inspected opportunities

The latest full CI has 12 unchanged-limit timeouts and Vitest 1354.37 s against 900 s. The isolated three runTask cases pass, whereas the routine-history case still exceeds 5000 ms at 6159 ms. Earlier functional CI passed in 927.33 s but did not satisfy the bound. These observations show variability and an outstanding performance failure; they do not establish a host cause or a correctness pass for all timed-out cases. The repaired browser campaign passes with 31 synthetic turns; it must not be modified to compensate for CI duration.

Three independent read-only reviews identified the following current-code opportunities. Counts are static, not a measured time saving:

- `dashboard/src/server/routine-service.test.ts:309` exercises one real service/runner run, then prepares twenty strictly synthetic unstarted denial receipts through twenty whole store transactions solely for history presentation. The final assertions require 21 retained records, latest 20 visible, one provider invocation/charge, unchanged ledger bytes and preserved original completion.
- `runner/workflow-orchestrator.test.ts:32` creates/authorizes a base issue that `correctionFixture` replaces with its own real issue. Its fifteen parameterized uses therefore have thirty unnecessary create/authorize preparation operations. The complete correction variants also repeat `detail()` projections between the same acts. A conservative reviewed mapping suggests 30 -> 16 test-owned reads per complete variant, including an additional fresh read after restart/reconcile/runOnce; the implementation must measure the actual count and retain stricter freshness over that numerical target.
- `lib/state/maintenance.ts:136` enumerates every own descriptor at each prototype level solely to test whether `then` exists. A single own descriptor lookup can express the same condition without invoking getters. Privately retaining only immutable lexical prefixes could also remove repeated string work, but neither opportunity removes fresh filesystem work or demonstrates gate-sized savings.
- Candidate inspections are not expendable setup: an inspected successful tree path uses fifteen real Git operations, and the three current fences for each successful read-only Reviewer/QA turn remain real. Four such turns in a full correction imply 180 operations on that static route, before other Git work. No runtime inspection, protected probe, role, verification, reconstruction or lease check may be cached/skipped to reach the bound.

### Exact proposed target delta

| Path | Proposed responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `scripts/profile-maintenance.ts` (new) | Fixed synthetic profiling workloads, phase/operation counters, sanitized measurements and verified owned cleanup; no caller-provided project/state paths or arbitrary commands. |
| `scripts/profile-maintenance.test.ts` (new) | Profiler isolation, transparent observation, failure propagation, output privacy and cleanup tests; no expensive profile campaign in default CI. |
| `dashboard/src/server/routine-service.test.ts` | Only the history case's synthetic preparation; keep its real run and every existing assertion/time limit. |
| `runner/workflow-orchestrator.test.ts` | Explicit/lazy creation of the actually used issue and consolidation of test observations between acts; keep all real role/Git/verification paths and existing waits. |
| `lib/state/maintenance.ts` | Only measured, guard-equivalent CPU work: single `then` descriptor lookup and, conditionally, private lexical prefix plans. |
| `lib/state/maintenance.test.ts` | Equivalence/fault regressions for the preceding computational changes; every original guard assertion and timeout remains. |

This design and `.agents/specs/tasks/phase-p-operational-continuity.tasks.md` receive proposal/evidence updates only. Four new implementation paths would be added to the existing nineteen-file T015 scope after exact approval: the two profiler files and the two fixture test files. The corrected T015 total would then be twenty-three paths, not authority to modify any other file. `runner/run-task.test.ts` is observed/revalidated unchanged: its three isolated passing cases have no established safe shortcut worth adding to scope. No store, service, runtime caller, Git adapter, schema, dependency, registry, UI/browser script, Harness validator/catalog/pin or spec approval metadata change is proposed.

### Ordered draft substeps

1. **T015-P01: establish measurements first.** Implement/test the two profiler files after scope approval. Use unique verified OS-temp roots and fictional validated records. Measure preparation, action/verification, assertion observations and cleanup separately without moving preparation outside a test deadline. Fixed workloads include synchronous boundary entry, 100 task mutations and 100 event appends, with absent and valid null-hold control cases separated. Use three serial samples per selected workload; report median and maximum, source hashes, operation counts and exact cleanup. Representative unchanged fixture workloads are the routine-history case, three T007 stop variants, two complete corrections and lost-callback recovery. Selection is a closed code-owned enum, not an arbitrary test filter, command or path. Run these workloads in separate invocations rather than silently allocating another deadline inside one campaign. Instrumented counts and uninstrumented wall samples are distinct; stack attribution is a separate non-timing pass if needed.
2. **T015-P02: remove redundant fixture preparation.** Preserve the routine create/configure/run/idle path. Construct the same twenty denial entries in memory over a detached final document, preserving IDs, clock advances, slots, the twenty revision increments and final timestamp. Validate each occurrence and the complete aggregate before one test-only historical seed, after the real runner is idle and without concurrent writers. Preserve the original completed entry, definitions, cursors and ledger bytes. This is explicitly fixture bootstrap, not twenty genuine executions or production batch admission. The current store requires at most one new occurrence and its exact transaction time; grouping twenty through a production transaction would violate that contract and is rejected. The real detail reader and all original assertions still run against native files within the same test/time bound.
3. **T015-P03: reduce duplicate workflow observations.** Common setup retains a unique root, complete public configuration and independent files. Create/authorize the base issue explicitly only when used; every correction still creates and authorizes its own real issue. Consume `advance()`'s real post-reconciliation result for assertions at that same point rather than rereading repeatedly. Any decide/reconcile, Git/source write, restart, worker tick or mutation attempt invalidates a snapshot as current state, even when the act is a no-op or throws. Historical snapshots remain comparison values, never authority. Preserve all seven role runs, current-candidate inspections, negative/protected/reconstruction probes and the existing 5000/15000/90000 ms limits. Do not replace polling with a completion shortcut or add `runAttempt` injection in this first supplement.
4. **T015-P04: retain only demonstrated computational wins.** Replace full descriptor enumeration by an own `then` lookup only after equivalence cases pass: own/inherited/hidden/undefined/getter properties, Proxy/Promise/Generator, prototype traversal and uncertain-frame retention. Evaluate lexical plans only if P01 shows meaningful repeated string work. Plans contain immutable absolute path/prefix strings privately tied to the lease/frame, never Stats, bytes, absence, parsed control, validity, ACL facts or TTL authority. Relative inputs still resolve in their current context before any private lookup. All current ordered fresh lstat samples, independent owner O_RDONLY reopening/fstat/read/close, root/owner late samples, token comparison, current hold read and release/FD error behavior remain. Compare fault outcomes and sanitized operation/argument/flag traces before retaining a change; remove a neutral/negative experiment using only its own reversible delta.

The profiler runs only fixed local fake/state workloads and bounded existing test selectors. It strips external-provider/runtime credentials, performs no paid turn, provider read, publication or real sibling operation, and cannot accept a user state path. Observation wrappers preserve arguments, return values and thrown error identity; instrumentation never bypasses a guard. It prints aggregate counts/timing/hash/cleanup evidence, not owner tokens, record bodies, raw errors, source snippets, prompts or stacks. It closes only its owned resources, verifies containment/non-links before removing its own root, restores environment and preserves uncertain cleanup evidence on failure. A failing sample remains nonzero/failed with partial measurements, not a successful benchmark. Unit tests exercise its control/isolation with small fakes; default CI never embeds the expensive repeated campaign.

Each profiler invocation has one explicit 900-second maximum wall deadline beginning before setup/import/launch and never restarted by a sample or child. Reserve the last 30 seconds within that same ceiling for bounded shutdown/cleanup; stop launching work when that reserve is reached. Supervise only child handles actually issued by that invocation, preserving the cases' existing 5000/15000/90000 ms limits. On expiry, fail with partial measurements, stop only proven owned active handles and await bounded exit; never kill unrelated/unknown processes by PID or age. Missing exit/descendant/writer evidence retains the owned fixture with cleanup unavailable instead of deleting live state or reporting success. The ceiling is a proposed diagnostic bound, not a change to the Harness default, existing behavior gate, test timeouts or production deadlines. Profiler tests must cover a stalled child, deadline across successive samples, exhausted cleanup reserve and refusal to remove a fixture with uncertain live writers.

### Verification after exact implementation approval

No further manual acceptance is delegated. Run serially, not alongside a browser/CI/benchmark campaign, and never stop or reconfigure the owner's ordinary local processes:

1. `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/profile-maintenance.test.ts --maxWorkers=1`, then `pnpm exec tsx scripts/profile-maintenance.ts` for the fixed default core workload before/after each retained optimization. The script's finite workload enum maps to the representative fixture selections above; every invocation uses the same total wall deadline and unknown arguments/paths are refused. These future commands are not executable now because the proposed files do not exist yet. Report failing/unfinished baseline samples honestly; do not lift their limits to collect a success.
2. Repeat the history case and all twelve previously failing cases with their original assertions and limits; then run the complete `dashboard/src/server/routine-service.test.ts`, `runner/workflow-orchestrator.test.ts` and unchanged `runner/run-task.test.ts`. Name-filtered cases are not passes. No removal of parameter variants, real fixtures or restart probes is permitted.
3. Run all six core-store files with `SURTEC_MAINTENANCE_BROWSER_SMOKE=1` and restored process environment in finally, plus the current 37 browser helper/failure-safety cases. Preserve raw-byte/hold/FD/late-callback faults, real API restart/session denials and responsive browser checks. Report new counts rather than predicting them from historical 564-case acceptance.
4. Freeze source, run `pnpm schemas:check`, `pnpm typecheck`, the original `pnpm ci:check`, `pnpm audit:check` and fresh complete `pnpm smoke:local:playwright`. The existing full-suite worker configuration and test deadlines remain; report Vitest, whole-command wall, skipped opt-ins and actual cleanup separately. The approved behavior check still has the existing 900-second per-command ceiling; no failed/slow/partial run becomes acceptance. Require actual bounded passing evidence, not a favorable microbenchmark or a reduced test selection.
5. Update evidence and run Harness/secret/whitespace checks. Only then may T015 be checked and its exact scope locally committed. No push/merge, full-P acceptance, T016 implementation or production activation follows automatically.

### Rejected approaches and stop conditions

Do not reuse an already open owner FD to remove permission-reopening checks; cache filesystem facts or policy/context results; remove/fuse guards, snapshots/events or store RMW boundaries; batch production occurrences; fake successful roles/receipts; shorten histories/probes; move expensive setup into hooks merely to evade the case timer; change root/temp layout, heap, worker count, timing/caps, HTTP bounds or security software to manufacture acceptance. No new infrastructure or database is justified by this diagnosis.

None of the static savings or CPU options currently proves the required reduction from 1354.37 to 900 s. P01 is a decision gate, not a promise. If the unchanged safety contract dominates the measured cost or the exact supplement remains insufficient, stop with the measured breakdown and a separately reviewed scope/design proposal; do not repeatedly launch CI blindly, weaken requirements or declare T015 done. Production readiness stays partial and snapshot/cutover false throughout this corrective work.

### T015-P01/P02/P03 measured implementation checkpoint - 2026-10-13 local

The fixed profiler uses three uninstrumented wall samples and one separate instrumented pass per core/control combination. Its single process-origin 900-second ceiling includes startup, children and a final 30-second cleanup reserve. A separate outer watchdog remains responsive when its controller is blocked. Only issued child handles and privately proven immediate OS-temp roots are supervised; an uncertain descendant/writer or incomplete root identity prevents deletion. Aggregate output has closed numeric operation keys, fixed public source hashes and cleanup evidence, never raw child errors, record bodies or owner tokens. These source hashes cover the declared observation set, not the entire transitive dependency graph.

Independent review found that transparent observers could silently omit failed clock/count/record observations. The corrected private latch preserves the original return/error identity but refuses measurement acceptance; writer quiescence is proven separately for safe owned cleanup. Successful instrumented passes require non-null durations and matching positive-count keys, while legitimate zero durations remain valid. The source freeze is profiler `376b3a6c111df60308898cf334161d3a88d1f227711f8e25ec40076922203aa3` and test `c9c0421748a3472f5f623f90c0e516c47db2a837b0bdace3343d627972d34d28`. The initial core campaign completed 24 jobs in 175.174 s with no source drift and complete cleanup. | `runner/workflow-orchestrator.test.ts` | Add a context argument and one best-effort synchronous capture at the start of its existing root `afterEach`, selected only for the exact implementation case. Preserve the remainder of the hook and every case body. |
| This design and `.agents/specs/tasks/phase-p-operational-continuity.tasks.md` | Proposed scope, actual approval if later given, results, limitations and stopping conditions only. |

These are five existing paths inside the current T015 scope, not five additional targets. No new reporter file, runner override, Vitest configuration, dependency, schema, store, API/UI, registry, Harness pin/script/catalog, snapshot or runtime caller is included. No constructor/fixture/`complete()` parameter or body instrumentation is needed.

#### Verified installed API and observation point

Primary evidence is the installed **Vitest 4.1.11** implementation and declarations, inspected without executing tests:

- `node_modules/.pnpm/@vitest+runner@4.1.11/node_modules/@vitest/runner/dist/chunk-artifact.js:2965`: callback/before-hook failures are recorded by `failTask`; `runner.onTaskFinished` then runs before `afterEach` (2969-2974). `onTestFinished` and `onTestFailed` run after hooks/fixture cleanup (2985-2988).
- `tasks.d-DEYaIMIu.d.ts:1282` exposes `TestContext.task`; `:550` declares optional `TaskResult.errors`; `:1221` types the `afterEach` context. `createTestContext` in `chunk-artifact.js:2348` constructs a **function** with its own `task` property: do not reject the context merely because it is not a plain object.
- `chunk-artifact.js:3068` appends processed errors, including flattened AggregateError entries. `:2261`/`:2404` implements and generates timeout errors. Timeout rejection does not forcibly terminate the callback promise. Final case duration is assigned only at `:3052`.
- Nested `afterEach` hooks can precede the root hook (`chunk-artifact.js:2610`); the reporter's `onTestCaseResult` is documented as post-test/post-hooks (`node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/reporters.d.DtoKVV2s.d.ts:1083`). Neither is a guaranteed pre-cleanup interception point.
- `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/coverage.DM_a_rWm.js:538` resolves Node-mode defaults to test timeout 5000 ms and hook timeout 10000 ms. The current `vitest.config.ts` does not override either. These are distinct existing bounds, not proposed timeout changes.

Use the supported context parameter on the **existing** root `afterEach`, not a custom runner, additional hook, abort listener or reporter. At its first statement, inspect the exact test binding and project the already recorded result **before** `fsTrace.finish()`, `worker.stop()`, environment/mock restoration or root removal. Label it `start-of-existing-root-afterEach` / `pre-cleanup-framework-result`, not "before any cleanup", "callback-only failure" or "final result". BeforeEach, `runner.onTaskFinished` and earlier internal hooks may already have contributed errors. Later cleanup failures are outside this snapshot and must not be inferred from it.

Read-only contract fingerprints at design time: runner `chunk-artifact.js` `BF6AF6D2F4469911879A67E83B8F9E7B20332CF42795032C53457B10CCC1C617`; runner declarations `53CF4076F42B29B8D411259D168D51B3A0274C42C8814E5B44DFA8803A35D4FC`; Vitest package metadata `A28126D97BCAF567DA5BED69443B7F3BCD9A7A8C38C8B66E554686B6BB2C10E0`. These cover the declared evidence files, not the entire dependency graph, and are not new registry pins. A changed installed contract requires reinspection; version/display names alone cannot authorize reuse.

#### Selection, transparency and closed receipt

Propose one distinct future selector `--workload=first-planner-failure` and child-only flag `SURTEC_TEST_PLANNER_FAILURE_PROFILE=1`. Neither exists yet. The flag is stripped from general inherited environments and added only by that fixed diagnostic launcher, which strips both legacy flags. P08 capture abstains if either P05/P06 flag is present: conflicting flags yield unavailable **P08** evidence, not a global guarantee that legacy instrumentation did not run. Existing P05/P06 activation and case bodies remain byte-for-byte unchanged. The original CI/default profiler selections remain unchanged; P08 is silent when disabled.

Selection must match the exact current module path and literal implementation-case name from trusted runner context; do not enumerate other test bodies or follow suite/context cycles. Validate repeat/retry counters as the original single attempt, not a replacement sampling policy. Mismatched or changed bindings cannot produce a selected-case receipt. Source hashes are collected outside the hook; there is no new filesystem, task/workflow, executor, Git or provider read inside the capture.

Inspect only named own data descriptors along `context.task`, `task.name`, `task.file.filepath`, `task.result`, result state/counters/errors, error name/message/stack and numbered error-array entries. The framework context may be a non-Proxy function; result/error payloads must have supported plain/null-prototype shapes. Reject Proxy, accessor, sparse or malformed data without invoking getters, `toJSON`, custom methods or recursive serialization. Never rerun Vitest's `processError`, which can format/mutate error payloads. Do not change/freeze the original context/result/errors; freeze only a detached projection.

The proposed receipt has closed keys only:

- `schema_version: 1`, `kind: planner-failure-observation`, `variant: implementation`, fixed observation-point tag and `acceptance: false`.
- `framework_state: pass | fail | unavailable`; this is a snapshot at hook entry, not final case or domain/task state.
- `error_count`: exact integer 0..8, or null when unavailable. A supported pass with no recorded errors can report zero; fail with missing/empty errors, more than eight entries, holes or contradictory counters becomes unavailable rather than silently truncated.
- Ordered entries with index, a closed reported-name/category enum, an exact recognized-message signature or `unsupported`, a regenerated canonical public message or null, and optional reported fixture-source positions (at most three **across the entire receipt**, not three per error). An unrecognized exception is a limited observation with `classification_complete: false`, never a fabricated timeout or arbitrary text echo.
- `capture_status: captured | unavailable`, separate `classification_complete`, and a closed unavailability reason. Missing/invalid capture never means pass or no failure.

Recognize only the **complete** installed Test-timeout message for the unchanged 5000 ms case bound and Hook-timeout message for the unchanged 10000 ms hook bound, not a prefix or elapsed-time guess. Emit the matching fixed signature and regenerate only its public first sentence. Other timeout values are unsupported, not silently adopted configuration. Name equality or a matching message is a **reported signature**, not unforgeable provenance, a verified causal mechanism or justification to relax a limit. Non-timeout names may be mapped to a small closed set (`Error`, `AssertionError`, `TypeError`, `RangeError`, `ReferenceError`, `SyntaxError`, `AggregateError`, unknown); omit their arbitrary messages, values, diff, cause, expected/actual and custom fields.

Before any regex/split, bound candidate messages to 1024 UTF-16 units and UTF-8 bytes and raw stack input to 65536 units/bytes per error. Scan at most 128 stack lines per supported error, emitting at most three fixed `workflow-fixture` tokens across the entire receipt with bounded positive line/column numbers; never paths or method names. Positions must fit the frozen public fixture source limits. Optional positions are a bounded selection, not a claim to include every frame; reaching that limit is explicit. Unsupported or oversized optional stack data makes positions unavailable; it does not convert an unknown exception into success. Timeout stacks may identify test registration rather than the blocking operation: label positions "reported source sites", not causal sites. No raw error/message/stack, owner token, private record, model text or normal-state path is persisted or forwarded.

#### Immediate transport and separate execution evidence

Attempt exactly one native `PLANNER_FAILURE_PROFILE` line at hook entry after projection, at most **8192 UTF-8 bytes**. Do not await stream drainage, add a quiet period, install process/abort/error listeners, write an evidence file or wait on a domain operation. Synchronous projection/emission faults are caught without rethrowing or replacing the original test/cleanup error; the original cleanup statements still run in their original order. Observation adds bounded work to the diagnostic hook and is not claimed to have zero overhead.

Transport is best-effort: `stdout.write` returning or a synchronous try/catch does not prove delivery, flush, detection or neutralization of all asynchronous pipe errors. A killed/blocked worker may never reach the hook or publish a full marker. Detected transport faults and missing/truncated/invalid markers make capture unavailable. A validated received snapshot can remain partial evidence when later child/source/cleanup facts are uncertain; those execution facts remain independently unavailable. Backpressure is not flush or success, and no transport event is reported as test acceptance. No raw output/file-state snapshot is a fallback.

The parent consumes only the fixed marker with a byte-bounded incremental parser and strict closed DTO validation. Reject duplicates, duplicate JSON keys, extra fields, overlong or truncated lines and inconsistent state/error counts. A second malformed marker revokes earlier progress. Immediately forward only the sanitized detached receipt through the existing private controller IPC; discard raw child stdout/stderr as in P06. Preserve a valid received early observation if the later controller/workload expires, but keep suite, task terminal state, source-after proof and cleanup unavailable when unproven. A later pass/failure cannot retroactively rewrite the early snapshot.

Any future loaded diagnostic runs the **original complete Vitest workload** with its existing two workers and test/hook/poll/HTTP bounds; no name filter, bail, repeat, retry, timer replacement, callback wrapper or changed preparation boundary. Its one existing process-origin 900-second ceiling and final 30-second cleanup reserve are shared by all issued children. Continue original work after the marker rather than stopping a case or manufacturing cleanup. Only actually issued handles may receive bounded stop attempts; unknown descendants/roots are never killed or deleted. Normal suite completion, final actual case result, source hashes and cleanup are recorded independently, with `diagnostic_only: true` and `acceptance: false` throughout. A captured early error or a diagnostic-only pass cannot mark T015 done.

#### Verified API and identity lifetime

Installed primary source remains Vitest 4.1.11 with the unchanged P08 fingerprints. `chunk-artifact.js:2009-2033` wraps `it.each` handlers as `handler(...items)`; `tasks.d-DEYaIMIu.d.ts:691-699` likewise exposes only tuple arguments. Do not invent a third context argument, replace `it.each` with `it.for`, or install a runner override. `chunk-artifact.js:2921-2948` sets `retryCount: 0` and `repeatCount` before passing `test.context` to `beforeEach`; the existing hook-context API is the supported binding point.

Add only a context parameter and best-effort preparation statement to the existing `beforeEach`, before its original filesystem/setup statements. Preparation reads named own descriptors for the exact private task object, fixed module/case and zero counters. It invalidates the current slot on **every** before-hook invocation, then binds a new opaque handle in a private `WeakMap` keyed by that exact task object only for the selected case. It does not start a clock, emit, inspect domain state, or do source filesystem work there; public source bounds are prepared once outside hooks only when the new flag is enabled. A failed before-hook or missing binding cannot invent a callback start.

At the first statement of the selected callback, synchronously consume the prepared slot once and retain the returned handle in a local constant before any original operation or await. This relies on the current non-concurrent selected case; detect observed overlapping/duplicate preparation/start and fail unavailable rather than changing concurrency. An overlap invalidates a still-active earlier handle; an ordinary later case does not invalidate an already sealed historical snapshot. Selection is not based on `template` alone: trusted preparation and later capture must match the exact private task identity, module, literal case and original counters. Do not persist that identity or a domain ID. The slot is not used again by span methods or late continuations.

Explicitly propose adding an **optional final test-observer argument** to `ensureBaseIssue`, `fixture` and `complete`, passing this same local handle through the selected helper chain. Existing arguments keep their order/default behavior; production constructors are untouched. This is an intentional proposed test-helper signature delta for human review, not already approved by P08. All helper `begin/end` closures reference their local handle, never a globally replaceable cursor. The root after-hook resolves the handle through its exact task-keyed WeakMap, not the current slot; do not clear that map when another before-hook runs. Late callback activity retains only its sealed original handle, cannot mutate another case or publish another marker, and must not read the clock after sealing.

#### Observation window and span contract

Use a new cursor, not `createPlannerPhaseRecorder().receipt()`: the P05 method seals, requires cleanup and does not retain an active span's elapsed time. Do not change its historical meaning. Begin/end calls are best-effort timestamp statements surrounding the same original operations; never execute/await an action callback on behalf of the fixture. Observer faults return no-op closers and cannot replace original thrown errors or delay/skip the original operation/cleanup.

Observe the eleven existing body labels in their existing order: `issue-create`, `issue-authorize`, `preview`, `admission`, `dispatch`, `terminal-observation`, `assertions`, `probe-reconcile`, `probe-worker-tick`, `probe-restart-reconcile`, `probe-assertions`. Keep real `worker.runOnce()`, `vi.waitFor(..., { timeout: 5000 })`, every assertion and restart/tick probe unchanged. Add only the matching observer starts and finally-based ends at those public boundaries. There are no nested spans or more than one open span; duplicate, reversed or overlapping observations invalidate timing rather than manufacturing a total.

The proposed `span_cursor` has these closed fields only (`phases` is null for the explicit unavailable variant, never a fabricated zero-filled table):

- `measurement_scope: callback-entry-observer-to-root-afterEach`, `timing_status: captured | unavailable`, `unavailable_reason: null | case-binding-unavailable | callback-start-not-observed | invalid-clock | invalid-span-sequence`, and `observation_elapsed_ms: number | null`.
- A dense table of exactly the eleven fixed body labels. Each row has `entries: 0 | 1`, `state: not-observed | open | ended`, and relative `start_ms`, `end_ms`, `elapsed_ms` as finite numbers or null with strict coherent combinations. Not-observed means no marker was recorded, not proof that domain work did not occur. Ended means a finally boundary was observed, not that the operation succeeded.
- `cursor_phase`: one body label, `between-public-spans`, or null when unavailable; `last_ended_phase`: one body label or null; `between_spans_ms` and `cursor_elapsed_ms`: bounded relative numbers or null. Record unattributed between-span intervals directly at transitions/snapshot using the same monotonic origin, not a fabricated CPU/FS residual. `cursor_elapsed_ms` measures the current open span or current gap since its observed start; `between_spans_ms` accumulates only the observed gaps. Unknown/incoherent arithmetic stays null/unavailable, never clamped to zero.
- Fixed `cleanup: not-observed-at-this-point` and `sealed: true`. No body-end marker, promise-resolution claim, domain status, arbitrary label, absolute timestamp, command, path, task/workflow/owner identifier, raw error/stack, model/provider text or record body is added.

For `captured`, the reason is null and the phase table is dense: a not-observed row has entries 0 and all times null; an open row has entries 1, a valid start, null end and elapsed to the snapshot; an ended row has entries 1 and valid ordered start/end/elapsed. At most one row is open. For `unavailable`, the reason is required and `phases`, all numeric fields, `cursor_phase` and `last_ended_phase` are null; retain only the fixed scope/cleanup/seal fields. This does not claim that earlier markers never occurred. Available observation offsets are monotonic in recorded order; offsets and individual durations are finite, nonnegative and at most the unchanged 900000 ms ceiling. Each duration follows its own offsets, with no monotonic ordering between durations of different phases. The validator reconstructs the ordered nonoverlapping partition from offset zero through the snapshot, including ended/open spans and gaps, and recomputes duration/gap aggregates with the same canonical arithmetic; individually finite but contradictory totals or last/current phase fields are rejected. Do not require naive floating-point summation equality or report rounding as additional work. Missing starts/ends are null, not zero. An absent callback marker or thrown/regressive/non-finite clock makes timing unavailable permanently, without discarding a separately valid P08 failure classification. Do not fall back to wall-clock time or invent a duration.

Call this an **observation window**, not exact callback duration or time remaining in the 5000 ms timer. It includes observation overhead, unmarked constructors/request/snapshot reads and possible framework/nested-hook delivery before the root after-hook; those gaps are unattributed. An open span means its end marker had not been observed at this snapshot, not a proven blocking operation, still-live task or cause. Do not compute `5000 - elapsed`, infer CPU/FS percentages, sum overlapping intervals, or treat the registration source site as causal.

#### One atomic envelope and bounded transport

Propose a distinct selector `--workload=first-planner-spans`, child-only flag `SURTEC_TEST_PLANNER_SPAN_PROFILE=1`, and single native marker `PLANNER_SPAN_PROFILE `. The fixed launcher strips all three old Planner flags and adds only the new flag. New capture resolves enablement/conflicting flags before any source-bound preparation or clock; it abstains if any P05/P06/P08 flag is present. Old paths remain unchanged and do not acquire the new flag through inherited environments. Default/disabled operation performs no timing, source loading or output. This design has not created any of these selectors/flags/functions yet.

As the first diagnostic statement of the existing root `afterEach(context)`, seal exactly one combined envelope before the existing P08 capture, `fsTrace.finish()`, worker stop, mock/env restoration or root removal. P08 capture is disabled under the new fixed launcher, so it emits no second marker. The envelope has exactly `schema_version: 1`, `kind: planner-span-observation`, `variant: implementation`, `observation_point: start-of-existing-root-afterEach`, `result_scope: pre-cleanup-framework-result`, `acceptance: false`, the **unchanged nested P08 `failure_receipt`**, and `span_cursor`. Obtain both projections synchronously in that one observation turn for the same task identity. Freeze only copies, never original context/results/handle state owned by the framework. A valid failure can accompany explicitly unavailable timing; an identity mismatch cannot be presented as correlated evidence. Later callback closers are ignored after sealing and never retroactively alter the historical snapshot.

Emit at most one best-effort write of at most 8192 UTF-8 bytes **including prefix/newline**. Test the largest allowed envelope (eight P08 errors, three total public sites, eleven span rows). If it cannot fit, emit no marker and retain envelope unavailability in the parent; do not send a second fallback write, silently truncate or print raw data. The maximum is a proposed gate to prove, not a measurement already obtained. Preserve native-write/backpressure/asynchronous-EPIPE limitations from P08. No evidence file, await/drain, new hook/listener/timer, abort observer or quiet period is introduced.

Use the existing source-metadata pattern: one closed private handshake of fixture hash and bounded frozen public line limits from the same source buffer, before progress, matching the controller's before-source hash. The outer watchdog performs no filesystem work. The new collector validates UTF-8 and byte limits incrementally; reject duplicate/escaped JSON keys, extras, contradictory rows, missing/truncated/oversized markers and duplicate markers. Private IPC immediately validates/detaches the entire envelope; its first canonical identity is immutable, and null/malformed/changed/out-of-order/duplicate-source/wrong-hash progress revokes the whole envelope permanently. This transport revocation is distinct from harmless ignored late calls on an already sealed local recorder. A received valid snapshot may survive later expiry, without invented final suite/case, source-after or cleanup facts.

#### Question and minimal future scope

Observe which public suite/module/case boundaries were delivered before the deadline, whether the framework reported run end, and whether the issued Vitest process exited and closed its stdio. P09 does not establish that the first Planner caused the full-workload deadline. This observation is intended to distinguish missing execution progress, delivered run end without complete JSON, and process exit without stdio close; it does not promise a cause or a performance fix.
