These four paths already belong to the twenty-three-target T015 scope. No reporter/configuration file, dependency, package script, schema, registry, Harness artifact or new target is proposed. `runner/workflow-orchestrator.test.ts` is observed unchanged, not edited. Keep every P05-P09 body, selector, DTO, flag behavior, source list and default intact; add separate P10 helpers/branches rather than making their historical validators accept new meanings. No production state/runtime/UI change, fixture optimization, test removal, filter, retry, bail, worker/concurrency change, moved setup or altered timeout is proposed.

#### Verified primary contract and limits

The installed implementation is Vitest **4.1.11**. The [official reporter lifecycle](https://vitest.dev/api/advanced/reporters) distinguishes case results after hooks from run end; the exact installed files, not a newer online example, control implementation:

- `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/reporters.d.DtoKVV2s.d.ts:1040-1113` declares `onInit`, run start/end, module queued/collected/start/end and case ready/result. `onTestCaseReady` precedes before-hooks and can represent a skipped case; `onTestCaseResult` follows the test and its hooks. There is **no public `onExit`**. Do not use internal runner objects or experimental reporter APIs.
- `cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js:11424-11449` imports a custom reporter through `ctx.runner.import`, requires a default export and constructs it. `1252-1285`, `13176-13180` and `13244` establish the existing Vite module-loader path; `coverage.DM_a_rWm.js:434-451` retains both explicit CLI reporters. A default-export class in the existing profiler is statically compatible; its real TS-loader integration remains a future small-gate test, not an already observed pass. The existing `argv[1]` guard prevents an import from becoming a CLI/controller launch and must remain unchanged.
- `chunk-artifact.js:2814-2865` batches worker updates with a 100 ms throttle; `cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js:12657-12666` applies a batch's task results before dispatching its events. Reporter timestamps are **main-process delivery observations**, not original worker execution instants or exact case/hook durations. A ready callback may already expose a later result; do not read it as proof of a start-state. Interleaved workers have no inferred total execution order.
- `cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js:14050-14054` awaits reporters in `Promise.all`; `index.UpGiHP7g.js:3538-3630` generates/writes built-in JSON at run end. A reporter callback is not a guarantee that JSON is already complete, and an awaited custom operation would add scheduling delay. Use small synchronous callbacks with no await/drain, timer, global listener, test hook, filesystem/domain read, artifact write, new runner or teardown interception. Native enqueue still adds overhead and is best effort; do not claim zero perturbation, delivery/flush or asynchronous-pipe-error neutralization.
- `cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js:12668-12689` computes run-end reason without directly incorporating its separate unhandled-error array; built-in JSON success likewise does not directly count that array. Preserve only a bounded **unhandled-error count**, never the errors. Require that count to be known and zero for completion. `13998-14044` separates resource teardown/exit from reporting. The existing `superviseChild` observes the issued child's `close`; it is not a descendant/fixture cleanup census.
- Public `children.allTests()` supplies collection order. `TestCollection`/reported-entity registration at `cli-api.CnMVyzaz.js:11762-11830,12072-12116` keeps private object associations, but queued and collected can register different module wrappers. Preserve the roster key and permit that documented one-time transition, not arbitrary rebinding. `chunk-artifact.js:3076-3110,3135-3140,3181-3186` can deliver skipped case results without ready, including before module start on collection failure. These are valid negative-path observations. Paths, names, errors, metadata, framework IDs and task objects never become serialized wholesale.

Installed evidence SHA-256 pins for this design (unchanged source dependencies, not proposed edits): reporter declarations `7FA6FDC6EEEB3850E6B3166836F8C9199594C4F4BED3CEE02CAC0CA33D71894D`; CLI lifecycle/loader `A236001D048380E2C67D05423FC9EA3F26B07EE019BA8D6E622082F29D49102E`; built-in JSON `CDAFAF8E508042DDDBA72F8C7BBAE84E2836DDB6B06C7026520C35D91AF4E798`; CLI reporter configuration `E509F3CC1BD81AE6426265253FA926CBDE02BE5CEBFE35B1DF422017BFFDCA31`; runner delivery `BF6AF6D2F4469911879A67E83B8F9E7B20332CF42795032C53457B10CCC1C617`. Missing/drifted contracts require review, not an implicit dependency upgrade.

#### Fixed launcher, channels and private binding

Propose exactly `--workload=full-suite-progress`. Its fixed child argv is Vitest's existing `run --reporter=json` plus `--reporter=<absolute path of this profiler>`. No caller-selected reporter, command, path, filter or extra run is accepted. Leave global `vitest.config.ts`, two workers and all test/hook/runtime/HTTP/900-second limits untouched.

Build the environment from the existing minimal `profileEnvironment`, stripping inherited diagnostic/provider/user-state flags, then add only `SURTEC_TEST_SUITE_PROGRESS_PROFILE=1` and the existing `SURTEC_TEST_PLANNER_SPAN_PROFILE=1`. The latter deliberately preserves the P09 snapshot without a fixture/factory change; its current conflict rule rejects P05/P06/P08 flags, not this separate new flag. Historical launchers continue to discard the new flag. The default reporter class is inert when the exact new flag is absent or the required combination/conflict checks fail; resolve that before clock/source work/output. Import/constructor cannot launch anything. No normal test or historical selector acquires this reporter.

Keep P09's bounded native marker and original complete JSON on **stdout**. Send new `SUITE_PROGRESS_PROFILE ` lines only to **stderr**, one bounded best-effort native write per frame. Otherwise a run-end callback interleaved after JSON could invalidate the unchanged stdout parser. Drain both pipes continuously; discard non-marker stderr without logging, persisting or forwarding it. Never surface the raw JSON/error stream: retain its existing finite private buffer and export only validated aggregates. JSON remains necessary; the custom reporter never replaces it.

At run start, privately freeze the unique selected module roster from public specifications, bounded to 256 modules. Accept only lexical repository-contained, normalized ASCII relative test paths under the existing `cli`, `dashboard`, `lib`, `runner` or `scripts` source directories, at most 192 UTF-8 bytes, without traversal, virtual IDs, query/fragment, drive/UNC syntax or empty/dot segments. Match later module delivery to that exact selected project/specification/path binding, assign deterministic sorted module ordinals and keep that roster association immutable. Permit the documented queued-placeholder to collected-module object replacement once, before case identities are fixed; do not require the same wrapper object across those two callbacks or allow an arbitrary later replacement. Duplicate selected paths across projects/pools are unsupported observation, not merged runs. These are public diagnostic labels, not filesystem authority or proof against same-user forgery. Invalid/duplicate/unknown modules make observation unavailable without changing which tests Vitest runs.

At collection, assign 1-based local case ordinals from the bounded public `children.allTests()` traversal, storing exact module/case object associations privately. Keep at most 20000 case bindings and 64 unclosed ready/result boundaries; do not assume two workers imply two active cases. Never serialize `id`, `name`, `fullName`, parameters, tags, metadata, errors/stacks, absolute paths, domain IDs or records. Only the existing fixed first-implementation-Planner name may be compared privately in its exact module to assign the static alias `first-implementation-planner`; require exactly one such case before accepting a final Planner fact. Absence leaves that fact null, not fabricated; the tiny loader fixture intentionally has no Planner/P09 and must never qualify as the full diagnostic. On its result callback, inspect only the public named `diagnostic().retryCount`/`repeatCount` to establish zero original retries/repeats, nonzero or unavailable; do not serialize the diagnostic, infer attempts from callback count or claim the reporter and worker share a task object. Wrong identity, repeated association, contradictory sequence or capacity exhaustion cannot be silently reassigned/truncated or converted to success.

Collection/import failure may bypass collection/start/case events or deliver `case-result: skipped` before module start and without ready. Bind any such known cases by one bounded public collection traversal of their exact reported module, if available, without inventing a collected/start event; otherwise expose unavailable identity rather than guesses. Accept a single skipped result without ready for an already fixed private case; never fabricate an open boundary or a ready count. Permit the documented synthesized module start/end for early failure and subsequent normal run-end failure. The absence of those callbacks is not successful collection or zero tests. Later ready/result duplicates or rebinding remain invalid. Explicitly test collection, beforeAll and aroundAll failures as delivered by the installed contract.

#### Closed bounded progress and independent completion facts

Use a new closed frame union, not a permissive change to P09. Common fields are exactly `schema_version: 1`, `kind: suite-progress-observation`, `sequence`, `event`, `reporter_elapsed_ms`, `module`, `case` and `data`. Events are `reporter-init`, `run-start`, `module-queued`, `module-collected`, `module-start`, `case-ready`, `case-result`, `module-end`, `run-end`, `observer-unavailable`. Module is null or the closed `{ ordinal, source }` public-roster binding; case is null or `{ ordinal, alias }`, with alias null or the one static Planner alias. Kind-specific data is exactly: reporter-init/module-queued/module-start/case-ready null; run-start `{ module_count }`; module-collected `{ case_count }`; case-result `{ state, original_attempt }` with state passed/failed/skipped and original_attempt zero/nonzero/unavailable for the Planner or null for other cases; module-end `{ state }` passed/failed/skipped; run-end `{ reason, unhandled_errors }` with reason passed/failed/interrupted. Observer-unavailable has null module/case/time and exactly `{ reason }`, restricted to invalid-clock/invalid-binding/invalid-sequence/capacity-exhausted; it is terminal, conveys no raw error and cannot heal prior evidence. All counts/ordinals/sequences are finite safe integers within their respective caps (unhandled-error count 0..20000); no extra/missing keys or cross-kind fields. Do not inspect result state during ready. Unsupported repeated callbacks are observation failures; a single callback pair does not by itself prove no retries/repeats. Never change test configuration to fit an observer.

Start the reporter's monotonic origin only when its init callback is actually observed; require it before normal progress frames. Relative delivery times are finite, nonnegative, monotonic and at most 900000 ms. Missing init, a thrown/non-finite/regressive clock or contradictory offsets cannot become zero timing; only the explicit terminal observer-unavailable variant uses null time without reading the clock. Keep controller/watchdog receive times separately in their shared deadline domain; never subtract clocks from different origins. Any last-receipt age describes lack of received evidence, not a blocked/live test or a causal execution interval. Do not add periodic heartbeats solely to make a stalled suite look active.

Proposed hard diagnostic bounds: **2048 UTF-8 bytes per frame including prefix/LF; 50000 frames; 8 MiB total stderr bytes; 256 modules; 20000 cases; 64 unclosed case boundaries; a last-32-frame tail; 128 KiB for the complete sanitized final/IPC snapshot** including the P09 receipt. Prove maximum-cardinality/numeric-width envelopes in synthetic tests before a campaign. Reserve one frame and 2048 bytes inside the reporter's same count/byte budget for a single terminal observer-unavailable observation, then stop emission; that frame may precede init if binding/clock failed before init was observed. An actual synchronous native-write failure is not retried or followed by a fallback write, and asynchronous/delivery failures remain unknown. The controller independently limits all stderr bytes, including non-markers. Overflow changes only observation availability, not test selection, execution or deadlines; continue draining/discarding after overflow. No raw/truncated fallback frame/report or unbounded error channel is emitted. Missing delivery stays unknown and cannot satisfy completion.

Validate UTF-8 incrementally, exact fields and combinations, immutable roster/case bindings, consecutive sequence, monotonic delivery time, once-only lifecycle transitions and coherent counts. Reject duplicate/escaped JSON keys, truncated reserved prefixes including at LF, extra fields, replay, malformed frames and contradictory terminal facts. A transport contradiction revokes the corresponding progress availability permanently; later valid frames cannot heal it. Previous safe observations, if retained at all, must be explicitly historical/unavailable rather than advertised as current. Skip/todo cases remain skipped counts, not passes. Never fabricate missing totals or final state from a count plateau, a pre-cleanup snapshot, absence of output or a reporter success reason.

The new diagnostic remains `diagnostic_only: true`, `acceptance: false`. Its projected progress exposes observed module/case counts, immutable public module bindings, unclosed delivered boundaries, the last-32 safe frame tail and a nullable framework run-end fact. Counts distinguish collected, ready, finished, passed, failed and skipped; cumulative counts are bounded nonnegative integers and never decrease. Finished equals passed plus failed plus skipped, but can exceed ready on the legitimate skipped-without-ready paths. Maintain an explicit set of unclosed ready boundaries, not `ready - finished`. Open sets and the bounded tail may shrink/rotate legitimately; only cumulative counts/sequence/bound terminal facts are monotonic. Pending modules may be waiting, not executing; open case boundaries are delivery observations, not liveness evidence. Missing progress is null/unavailable, not an invented all-zero run. No complete per-case log or source/name catalog is persisted.

Keep these facts independent:

1. The unchanged P09 receipt, absent until actually received and immutable thereafter.
2. A public `onTestCaseResult` for the unique private Planner binding, after its hooks and with its named public retry/repeat counts known zero; still distinct from the worker's private identity and the overall module/run outcome.
3. Framework run-end reason and known unhandled-error count.
4. Complete, bounded, parseable original JSON and coherent aggregate/Planner results.
5. Main-process exit observed from one passive `once('exit')` on the **issued native child only**, including bounded code and normalized signal; no global/process/PID listener. This proposed diagnostic-only listener is explicit, does not change `superviseChild`, and cannot grant shutdown/cleanup authority.
6. The existing supervisor's independent stdio-close outcome and exit code, source-after comparison and narrow existing fixture-cleanup criterion.

The new controller first sends one closed source/bounds handshake tied to its before-source hashes. Use a separate fixed P10 source set: the eight existing diagnostic source paths, unchanged `vitest.config.ts`/`package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml`, and the five installed primary-contract paths above. Derive fixture bounds and its hash from the same source buffer. The outer watchdog performs no filesystem work. Progress before the Planner receipt is legitimate; use new validators/roles rather than P09's requirement for a receipt on every progress message. Validate/detach each IPC message immediately, immutable source/receipt bindings, consecutive sequence and nondecreasing cumulative counts/observed delivery offsets, while allowing explicit open sets and the tail to shrink/rotate. Missing/out-of-order/duplicate source, hash drift, null/changed previously bound receipt, contradictions or snapshot overflow fail unavailable. Parent/controller/source/stream identities are consistency evidence, not authenticated provenance against hostile same-user code.

The outer watchdog may retain only validated partial progress/exit facts after a later deadline, while unknown JSON, stdio close, source-after or cleanup remain independently null/unavailable. Do not erase a later fact merely because it arrived after run-end; JSON and native exit/close can legitimately arrive later. Conversely no case/module progress is allowed after its terminal run-end, and no changed terminal fact is accepted. For P10, reconcile the complete JSON through a separate closed projection requiring one unique `testResults` module per selected roster entry, no unknown/duplicate/missing module, and per-module `assertionResults` whose single statuses reconcile passed/failed/skipped (including todo/pending status normalization) with the observed case bindings/counts. `numTotalTestSuites` includes nested suites and is not a module count; do not equate them, add skip/todo twice or widen the legacy parser. A complete passing diagnostic additionally requires coherent full progress/run end, zero unhandled errors, a passing original-attempt post-hook Planner, a captured coherent P09 receipt, that complete passing original JSON, normal owned exit/stdio close with exit 0, no source drift and the existing narrow cleanup check. Run end alone, including reason passed, never meets that condition.

#### Separate questions and verified static evidence

| Question, not an established cause | Current source/evidence | Proposed additional observation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| How much synchronous attempt work occurs before terminal polling can progress? | P09 observed 3870.6668 ms across its first five completed phases. `workflow-orchestrator.test.ts:118-127` awaits `runOnce()` before `vi.waitFor`; `task-orchestrator.ts:728-825,863-913` selects/claims and launches asynchronously rather than awaiting task completion. | Attempt entry, immediate native-Promise return and settlement, plus entry/return of the existing fake executor body. These are inclusive boundaries, not exclusive attribution. |
| Are terminal reads expensive, sparse or still observing a non-terminal lifecycle? | Each existing callback calls `readTask` once (`workflow-orchestrator.test.ts:126`); `store.ts:114-124` reads, parses and validates synchronously. Installed `test.DNmyFkvJ.js:3359-3405` calls immediately, then uses its existing 50 ms interval and explicit 5000 ms timeout. | Aggregate timing/counts around that same read, first/last poll entry, largest entry gap and the same primitive lifecycle already needed by the assertion. No extra read or polling loop. |
| Can timeout leave work unobserved at the pre-cleanup snapshot? | Runner `chunk-artifact.js:2261-2346,2965-2975` rejects the timed wrapper/aborts the test context and proceeds to hooks without cancelling the original Promise. Existing `afterEach:54-61` captures P09 before `worker.stop()`; `stop():566-573` aborts its own controllers and waits for active promises. | One immutable P11 snapshot before stop. Record only what arrived before sealing; post-stop effects and later continuation remain unknown. Do not add cancellation or waits to establish them. |
| Which module delivery windows accumulate before the suite deadline? | P10 retained 58 end facts but only its last 32 frames; module rows contain no retained start/end offsets (`profile-maintenance.ts:954-960,1194`). Their durations cannot now be reconstructed. | Prospectively accumulate validated P10 module boundaries, keep bounded rankings and distinguish overlapping sums from observed interval union and whole-command wall time. |

The two 5000 ms limits have different starting points and scopes. Do not calculate an exact remaining case budget from P09's callback-relative observations. The site 194:118 identifies the historical `it.each` registration, not a blocked instruction. This fixture never calls `worker.start()`; its initially stopped worker does not enable the 250 ms polling loop or automatic successor ticks. Do not blame that poller, weaken repeated admission/context/lease checks, or infer a sole cause from the open terminal span. Observation overhead is part of the same limits and may affect a near-boundary run.

#### Axis A: one sealed Planner observation, not a new execution path

Propose the fixed selector `--workload=full-suite-attribution`. The worker handle and the public reporter alias remain separate consistency observations, not proof of a shared object or authenticated cross-process identity.

Add inert preparation in the existing beforeEach and consume the new observer inside the existing selected callback, alongside—not in place of—the P09 handle. Add exactly one capture **after both historical capture calls and before the existing worker stop** in the existing afterEach; preserve the historical calls' order. No new hook, timer, signal listener, heartbeat, OS probe, global monkeypatch, source copy, extra await or post-stop snapshot is proposed. After the final permitted capture-clock observation, mark the handle sealed **before constructing the detached snapshot**, then make its single best-effort write. All later notifications are no-ops before clock access/output and cannot mutate the historical snapshot or grant cleanup authority; freezing a DTO alone is not sufficient. A failed synchronous write is not retried; asynchronous delivery remains unproven until received.

Only when the bound observer exists, the fixture may supply the existing `runAttempt` injection. It must invoke the same real `runTask`, root, executor and exact default argument projection from `task-orchestrator.ts:547` (`{ ...runOptions, tracer: undefined }` for this fixture). Preserve every nested proof/signal reference; do not alter options or bypass the normal prepare/claim/execution guards. Return the **exact original Promise**. Observe settlement only on the native Promise produced by that call, with the native intrinsic and callbacks that ignore values/reasons and absorb observer failures; never normalize, await, replace, race or call an arbitrary thenable's getter. Side observation adds a Promise reaction/microtask, not zero perturbation. An unsupported return makes measurement unavailable without replacing the original return/throw. Normal/unselected fixture composition must omit this injection entirely.

Mark the existing fake executor's body entry and synchronous body return/throw, preserving its original result and async signature. A body return is not Promise settlement; attempt settlement is not task terminal persistence, workflow reconciliation, hook completion or cleanup. At the existing polling callback, read `readTask` and its lifecycle exactly once, feed only that already-required primitive to the observer, and keep the same `expect(...).toBe("finished")`. On a read failure record a fixed failure count, then rethrow the exact original error without reading/logging it. Keep `vi.waitFor` synchronous callback, 50 ms default, 5000 ms option and original outer case limit unchanged; do not introduce a first observation or move work outside the case to evade that limit.

The proposed closed `planner-wait-observation` receipt has schema version 1, static source/Planner alias, `observation_point: after-existing-captures-before-worker-stop`, `acceptance: false`, `sealed: true`, capture status/reason, its own nullable observation elapsed time, fixed checkpoint map/order and one aggregate poll object. It never contains a TaskRecord, signal/reason, option/result/error, ID, title/name, path beyond the static public source, payload or full per-poll history.

The exact checkpoint names are `callback-start`, `attempt-enter`, `attempt-return`, `attempt-sync-throw`, `attempt-resolve`, `attempt-reject`, `executor-enter`, `executor-body-return`, `executor-body-throw`, `wait-enter`, `wait-return`, `wait-reject`, `capture`: thirteen once-only named slots with null for unobserved times and a bounded unique-name order. Callback start is the actual origin (zero from that one clock observation); other finite nonnegative offsets are monotonic and at most 900000 ms. Enforce mutually exclusive return/throw, resolve/reject and wait-return/reject branches, prerequisite entry/return facts and no event after capture. Distinct worker, P09, reporter and controller origins are never subtracted or added to fabricate a remaining timeout or an exclusive cost breakdown.

Validate a **partial order**, not the list's written order: callback-start first and capture last; attempt-enter precedes attempt-return or sync-throw; attempt-return precedes resolve/reject; executor-enter precedes its body return/throw and belongs to the bound attempt; wait-enter precedes original poll calls and wait-return/reject. `runTask:701-702` can invoke the fake executor synchronously before its first await, so executor body events may precede attempt-return. Attempt settlement may arrive before or after wait-enter; do not impose a total order between these overlapping branches or require settlement to precede wait-return. Null facts remain partial, not reordered synthetic events. Add regressions for executor-before-attempt-return and settlement on both sides of wait entry.

Poll aggregates are exactly `calls`, `read_returns`, `read_throws`, `first_start_ms`, `last_start_ms`, `max_start_gap_ms`, `read_total_ms`, `read_max_ms`, `last_lifecycle`. Count at most **256** original callbacks, with at most one in-flight read; successful/failed reads reconcile with observed calls, gaps require two entries, and max/total values must match monotonic private observations. Lifecycle is queued/running/finished/missing/unsupported or null, never a raw property value. Zero counters are valid only after an actual bound callback start with no corresponding calls; missing start is unavailable, not an invented zero sample. Read time and entry gaps include observer/loop scheduling effects; neither identifies CPU, I/O, lock contention or worker delay causally.

Use one new native stdout marker `PLANNER_WAIT_PROFILE ` with a hard **8192 UTF-8 byte whole-line** cap, including prefix/LF; retain original P09 marker and complete original JSON on stdout and unchanged P10 progress on stderr. A clock/binding/sequence/capacity failure or unobserved callback produces only the closed unavailable form (`invalid-clock`, `invalid-binding`, `invalid-sequence`, `capacity-exhausted`, `callback-not-observed`), with measurements null. Exhaustion stops only observation, not polling/execution. Validate/detach own data descriptors before use, reject duplicates/escaped keys, malformed UTF-8/prefixes/truncation/extras, and retain immutable once-bound receipt semantics. Late code and post-stop causal questions intentionally remain outside this snapshot.

The new P11 stdout framer separates **only fully validated, LF-terminated, exact-prefix P11 receipt lines** from its private input to the unchanged original JSON reconciler. Preserve every other byte/order, including the P09 marker; do not broaden a P10 parser or remove substrings inside JSON. Count all stdout bytes, including separated markers, inside the same 8 MiB cap, and keep the original bounded channel private. Delayed valid receipt delivery after a complete JSON line may be separated without deleting JSON; malformed/reserved-prefix/truncated/duplicate/contradictory receipt transport revokes availability, cannot be discarded to manufacture successful JSON, and never grants completion. Arbitrary interleaving inside JSON remains a parse failure, not repair.

#### Axis B: prospective bounded suite delivery-cost projection

Consume only unchanged P10 frames already accepted by the existing framing and stateful identity/sequence validator. Privately retain at most **256** selected module bindings and received start/end offsets; no new worker instrumentation, perf API, timers or polling is needed for this axis. A received end without the required bound start is unavailable, not an inferred start. Queued/collected without start and start without end are distinct partial states. All P10 skips, errors, interrupted runs, batching, case identities and zero intervals retain their existing meanings.

Publish **top 16 ended modules**, including failed/skipped/zero-case modules and zero delivery intervals; sort descending interval then ascending ordinal. Also publish counts of eligible/retained/omitted ended modules, canonical **sum of received ended intervals**, and their **interval union**. Compute sums in ascending ordinal order and union in start/end/ordinal order so controller/watchdog reproduce identical finite values. Per-window and union bounds remain 0..900000 ms; the overlapping sum has its distinct mathematical bound **0..230400000 ms (256 x 900000)** and must not use the per-window validator. This is not a larger execution limit. Do not divide by two workers, equate sum with wall/CPU, hide failed modules, or infer exclusive setup/read/idle cost.

Also publish **top 4 start-without-end observations**, ranked by interval to the **last received reporter-frame offset**, then ordinal, with retained/omitted counts. This is an explicitly bounded ranking, not silent truncation or a complete active-process inventory. No start means no interval; no end is never filled from deadline, receive time or a later process-exit fact. Reporter horizon, ended-only union, overlapping sum and process-origin wall time have different scopes. These values and an empty open-case set do not establish activity, blocking, quiescence or a cause. P10's old sample cannot retrospectively populate the new accumulated rankings.

Add separate P11 loader/controller/watchdog/validators. Do not edit `runLoadedSuiteProgress` or pass a larger source dictionary to P10's closed validator. Each receipt kind emits at most one coalesced revocation; repeated malformed input is drained without additional IPC or healing. Reserving source + 50000 frames + two receipts/two revocations + exit + final requires at most **50007 messages**, below the existing 50008 ceiling. Prove the joint byte envelope rather than enlarging it or silently dropping another receipt, error, source or tail; overflow drains/discards and cannot change execution or cleanup authority.

Keep the original shared **900000 ms process-origin ceiling / 30000 ms reserve**, unchanged `ProfileDeadline`/`superviseChild`, original two workers and all case/hook/HTTP/runtime limits. Require original full coherent P10 pass, zero unhandled errors, original-attempt post-hook Planner pass, complete original JSON, known successful wait/attempt/native fake-body observations and finished lifecycle, all nineteen unchanged sources, normal owned exit/stdio close and the existing narrow cleanup criterion for diagnostic completed. Valid negative/partial receipts remain evidence, not successful completion. No global/PID/unknown-process termination, prefix cleanup, root adoption, security-software reconfiguration or cleanup waiver is proposed.

Require that flag and P11/P10/P09 flags all exactly 1, with conflicting P05/P06/P08 flags absent even when empty/0; reject accessors/Proxies before clock/output/source effects. Historical launchers continue stripping the new flag. No path, command, filter, timer policy or environment passthrough is accepted.

#### Axis A: target-module cases and coverage from unchanged P10 frames

Select exactly source `runner/workflow-orchestrator.test.ts` through the original validated module binding, never hardcode ordinal 133 or a case ordinal/title. Retain private ready/result offsets for at most the existing 20000 cases / 64 open boundaries. Preserve original identity/sequence/attempt/state checks. Only skipped results without ready already admitted by unchanged P10 count as unpaired results, not fabricated zero-length windows; non-skipped results without ready remain invalid. A ready without result is a distinct partial observation. Other module frames advance the same reporter horizon but do not become target cases.

Export a separate closed module projection: availability/reason, target binding and module-start/end/horizon state; original target-collected count and disjoint counts of paired-ended, ready-without-result, skipped-result-without-ready and unobserved cases. When collection is known, unobserved = collected - paired - open - unpaired; missing collection is not known zero. Export top **16** ended inclusive case windows, including failed/skipped/zero intervals, with eligible/retained/omitted counts, ordered duration descending then ordinal ascending. Each row contains only the existing case ordinal/Planner alias, result/original-attempt fact and finite offsets; no names, framework IDs, errors, paths beyond the fixed source or test records. Privately retain enough facts for both sides to derive the same output independently.

Compute the canonical **union of ended case windows**, validated as fully contained within the observed module window, and its **complement**. Out-of-bounds intervals invalidate the projection; never clip or repair them. Complementary intervals are only strictly positive gaps after canonical union: eligible count 0..20001, chronological interval ordinals 1..eligible, retained = min(eligible, 4), omitted = eligible - retained. Export complementary total and top **4** gaps ranked longest first then chronological ordinal. Complement means only time outside received completed-case windows, not idle time, setup, cleanup or exclusive work. It can include missing/open cases and batching. Use observed module end when present, otherwise last received reporter offset after a known start; do not fill a missing start or substitute deadline/receive/exit clocks. Every ready offset must lie within that same observed window. Export top **4** ready-without-result windows to that horizon with omissions; neither this ranking nor an empty one proves liveness/quiescence.

No new overlapping per-case sum is needed. Union/complement and all case/window offsets remain 0..900000 ms; coverage plus complement must reconstruct that observed module width using canonical start/end/ordinal ordering. That sort defines deterministic geometry, not execution order or ledger repair. Strict IEEE arithmetic can conservatively make an otherwise mathematically valid sample unavailable; preserve that limitation without tolerance, clamping or rounding repairs. Equal delivery times, touching/nested/overlapping windows and failed/empty/partial modules are legitimate when consistent with original frames. Invalid identity/order/clock/capacity or replay permanently makes this projection unavailable. It does not change execution or authorize cleanup. Reporter delivery intervals can be zero despite actual worker work; do not label them callback duration or consult diagnostic() to manufacture an exclusive measurement.

#### Axis B: one separate sealed Planner-continuity receipt

Use a new consumed WeakMap/context handle following the exact original Planner source/name/retry/repeat rules. Preserve P11 preparation and capture order. P12 begins only in the selected original callback, binds the same root/task/worker/controller/executor and original run options/proof references privately, and emits exactly once **after the historical captures and before the existing worker.stop** in the same afterEach. Equal-shaped/replayed contexts/instances/proofs are not adopted; no ID/proof/options/signal/result/error is exported. There is no new hook, post-stop snapshot, state read or early cleanup.

The new fixture-only method observers wrap only the exact existing controller instance's `release` and `reconcile`, installed once after the existing fixture admission/binding and before original dispatch. Do not modify any prototype/global, construct a replacement controller or install a runtime adapter. Read only own-data callable descriptors from the bounded known instance/prototype chain without invoking getters/Proxy traps; retain the original descriptor/method identity privately. Preserve `this`, every argument and exact original return/throw with one synchronous Reflect.apply call; no async wrapper, await, Promise normalization or duplicate invocation. A released proof may only be compared to the original runOptions.workflowExecution reference; do not inspect it or use it as new authority. Wrong receiver/binding/unsupported descriptor makes observation unavailable, not original execution denied. Partial installation may undo only a still-owned wrapper; never overwrite a changed/foreign descriptor or repair it.

After sealing, instance wrappers may remain inert on their fixture-owned object until disposal: any late caller still invokes the original method, with no observer clock, flag/signal read, logging, mutation or new output. Do not restore a method while worker cleanup may call it, add a cleanup hook or remove the controller's own reentrancy guard. Measurement entry/end describes the method invocation, not whether the guarded reconcile body performed work; a quick reentrant return is not an authority or liveness fact.

P12 uses one origin for callback start, attempt enter/return/native resolve/reject/sync throw, wait enter/return/reject, poll-body observations, method intervals and seal. Preserve the existing runAttempt injection's same real runTask/default `{ ...runOptions, tracer: undefined }` projection and exact original Promise/throw. Compose P12 as the outer observer around the unchanged P11 observeAttempt-to-real-runTask closure so P11 retains its original native reaction registration order. P12 adds one native reaction and its overhead; this is not a claim of no scheduling effect. Use captured native intrinsics and the P11-level unsupported-return/species/getter refusal, ignore resolved/rejected values and absorb observer failures. Never subtract P09/P11 offsets from P12 or require a false total ordering across overlapping wait/attempt branches.

Observe the existing synchronous poll body, including its original read and assertion, with entry and return/throw aggregates. Keep the original read exactly once, the original assertion/result/error, original synchronous callback return, 50 ms default and 5000 ms option/outer limit. No extra call, await, thenable probe, retry loop or task read after settlement. At most **256** original polls; at most **128 combined** release/reconcile invocations, with a bounded stack for reentrancy. Exhaustion stops measurement only. Per method/poll export entry/returned/threw/open counts, first/last start, last completed end and inclusive completed total/max. For open invocations export only count and maximum age to seal per method/poll, null age when none, never a list of up to 128 open calls or an invented end. Per method export up to **4** longest completed intervals with invocation ordinal and one settlement relation: before-settlement (end < settlement), after-settlement (start >= settlement), straddles-settlement (start < settlement <= end), or settlement-unobserved. Missing settlement never becomes before/after. Inclusive method totals can overlap/reenter; their separate arithmetic maximum is 128 x 900000 = 115200000 ms, not a larger execution limit or CPU time. Order and aggregates must reconcile with private observations.

Use at most **8 fixed once-only boolean snapshot slots**: first-poll-entry/exit, attempt-settle-after-first-poll, first-bound-release-entry/exit, first-post-settle-reconcile-entry/exit and seal. Later eligible repetitions do not overwrite a slot; conflicting binding makes P12 unavailable. Capture the original context's own-data signal reference once after rejecting Proxies/accessors, then read only aborted using the captured native AbortSignal.prototype getter with that original signal receiver. Never invoke an object override or inspect reason/stack/listeners; incompatible native brand makes observation unavailable. Resolve public vi.isFakeTimers through a verified callable data descriptor, without invoking an accessor/Proxy; query only inside/after the first actual poll callback, after waitFor has already queried it. Earlier timer samples stay null/not-yet-observed. Do not use getTimerCount, fake-timer internals, advances, restores, ref/unref, timer/global wrappers or event-loop probes. Use a per-handle sampler-in-progress guard as well as the clock guard; reentrancy, throw or non-boolean output fails only P12 measurement closed, preserving the original operation. Once sealed, even recursive notifications perform no sampler/clock work. A false mode flag is not proof a native interval was scheduled or live; true aborted at a boundary does not date the actual abort.

The new `planner-continuity-observation` schema-1 receipt is closed, static source/alias/observation-point, acceptance false and sealed true. Captured data includes fixed checkpoints/order, aggregates and boolean slots; unavailable reasons are fixed clock/binding/sequence/capacity/sampler failures with measurement fields null. Seal the handle after its final allowed observation and **before** constructing the detached DTO. Late notifications are inert even before clock/sampler access. One best-effort native `PLANNER_CONTINUITY_PROFILE ` stdout line is capped at **8192 whole UTF-8 bytes including prefix/LF**, with no retry on failed write. Existing P09/P11 outputs remain unchanged. This observation can show whether poll-body or post-settlement controller calls were seen/returned before seal, not why a timer failed to fire or what happened afterward.

#### Fixed transport, source binding, bounds and success semantics

P12 source metadata contains exactly the unchanged P11 **nineteen sources plus runner/workflow-orchestrator.ts**. Keep all six installed API pins and independent reviewed literals unchanged; the additional production file is dynamically before/after hashed and read-only, not a new executable/pinned dependency. Both sides independently replay original P10 progress/P11 costs and the new module-case projection. For historical reconstruction pass exactly the nineteen-source subset into the unchanged closed P11 validator, which retains its original sixteen-source P10 reconstruction. Never hand the larger dictionary or new fields to historical sanitizers, and never use exported top-N samples as full private evidence.

Add only a separate P12 closed compact final, not a full nested historical report or a full case catalogue. It carries original compact P11 facts/receipts/costs, the new module projection/continuity receipt and twenty-source before/after facts, independent original JSON/native exit/stdio close/receive/cleanup, diagnostic_only true / acceptance false and fixed limitations. Complete additionally requires original coherent P11 success plus valid available new observations, exact private bindings, source freeze and normal owned exit/close/cleanup. A valid captured negative or partial receipt remains evidence, not completion; no inference repairs missing polls, module/case boundaries or cleanup. Known zero needs actual observation; null is not zero.

Retain **every existing cap**: 2048-byte/50000 progress frames, 8 MiB each stdout/stderr, 20000 cases/64 open, tail32, 131072-byte whole final/IPC snapshot, current IPC aggregate byte ceiling and **50008 messages**. New stdout framing removes only fully validated LF-terminated exact-prefix P12/P11 receipt lines from private JSON input, preserving every other byte/order and counting all original bytes inside 8 MiB; P09 collection still sees the unchanged original stream. No repair of interleaved JSON or malformed/reserved/truncated/duplicate/escaped-key data.

To fit three receipts without increasing IPC, use at most one metadata handshake, original frame deltas, three once-bound receipt deltas, **one global coalesced receipt-transport revocation**, one passive native-exit fact and one final: 1 + 50000 + 3 + 1 + 1 + 1 = **50007** messages. The first malformed/contradictory receipt transport permanently revokes the new P12 family receipt set; clear those trusted receipt facts, drain later bytes, send at most that one revocation and never heal/re-emit. An honestly unavailable but well-formed capture is not malformed transport and can coexist with the other valid receipts. Module projection is pure derivation over existing frames: its unavailability is carried in the existing projection/final, never another IPC message. Original P05-P11 families retain their own independent semantics. Do not silently drop evidence to make an over-cap stream pass.

Prove the joint byte envelope before any campaign by actual joint serialization, not estimated per-field sizes: source handshake with 10000 bounded line columns/twenty hashes; maximum P10 tail, module top16/open4 and new case top16/complement4/open4; all three 8192-byte receipt slots; maximum counts/numeric widths; original stdout/stderr/IPC ceilings and whole final **including role wrapper** <=131072. Source line_columns belong only to the handshake, never the compact final. Retain all counts and omission facts; if the combined representation cannot satisfy an unchanged cap, return to human design review rather than enlarge a cap or cut an existing receipt/error/tail. Original ProfileDeadline/superviseChild, 900000/30000 ms, two workers, all case/hook/HTTP/runtime limits and narrow cleanup criterion stay untouched.

### T015-P13 proposed first observation-rejection design - 2026-10-15 local

**Current authority: design only.** The owner authorized investigating why P12 returned no retained evidence, with existing guards/timeouts and no further campaign. Current writable scope is only this design and `.agents/specs/tasks/phase-p-operational-continuity.tasks.md`. P12's exit 1, literal failed status, 870065.9427 ms, null observations and unavailable cleanup remain historical facts; no cause or acceptance is backfilled. This is a local diagnostic delta within T015, not another product entity or phase. T015/T016 stay unchecked.

#### Verified source boundary and useful distinction

Static inspection of `runSuiteWorkflowTimingDiagnosticWatchdog` (`scripts/profile-maintenance.ts:2266` onward) identifies the missing explanation. Its `unavailable()` helper retains source/progress/receipts only while `revoked` is false. Message guards and handler exceptions can set `revoked = true`; later messages are ignored, and a failed return then exposes the empty report. A different path can return without having received source/final evidence. The current record cannot distinguish these paths. Its empty fields do not prove which branch occurred.

| Existing site | Proposed bounded explanation |
| --- | --- |
| Envelope/order/count, source binding and delta timing checks in the parent message handler | First existing guard site that rejected a message, with safe counters and source/final flags |
| Frame sanitizer and the three independent progress/cost/workflow projections | Which original sanitizer/projection first refused the frame, preserving call order and short circuiting |
| P09/P11/P12 receipt checks, the valid global receipt revocation, exit and final-report bindings | Distinguish a rejected message from an accepted negative receipt/revocation; identify the existing final rejection site |
| Send/catch paths and the original terminal OR condition | Which original return branch was taken and the already obtained outer-controller supervision outcome |

The loader/controller worker already suppresses private exceptions and can finish without a deliverable final. P13 will not instrument that child or infer its error. A parent code such as final-sanitizer or missing-final narrows the boundary; it is not a recovered inner exception or Planner diagnosis.

#### Proposed future scope and compatibility

Only four future writable files are proposed: `scripts/profile-maintenance.ts`, `scripts/profile-maintenance.test.ts`, this design and the existing task plan. All are existing T015 targets. The workflow fixture, production controller/runtime/stores, dependencies, configuration, schemas, registry/Harness artifacts and pins remain read-only. Preserve the twenty-source list, all six installed API literals, nineteen-to-sixteen-source reconstruction and dynamic before/after hashing. No additional source is required.

Keep the existing fixed `full-suite-workflow-timing` selector and environment unchanged. Keep the P12 report, every historical sanitizer/DTO, IPC role/shape, emitter and workload closed. Add no IPC message, environment flag, CLI input, path, child hook, timer, listener, clock sample, state read, retry or cleanup action. The controller remains the same issued fork; the original `ProfileDeadline`/`superviseChild`, two workers and all case/hook/HTTP/runtime limits remain unchanged.

A new opaque, process-local diagnostic handle may be an optional third argument to the existing watchdog. The CLI creates and passes it only for the selected P12 workload; existing two-argument callers remain inert and retain their original report/return behavior. Bind a factory-owned handle once through private identity storage, without evaluating supplied getters/Proxies. Bind its side receipt to the exact original returned report reference; the factory reader accepts only that reference, never a lookalike or a supplied report's contents. A foreign/reused handle mints no observation for another invocation; the first sealed observation remains unchanged and reading a different report yields unavailable. These bindings never grant execution authority or alter the original launch, rejection, status, cancellation or return. No user-selected logger, file path or callback is accepted through the CLI/IPC or registered with the watchdog. A unit-only writer seam for the final bounded line may use a fake; it receives no message/report payload and cannot control original execution/status.

#### First rejection, terminal branch and privacy

Use two fixed slots: the first parent rejection and the terminal branch. Record the first rejection immediately at its original guard/catch site, before its evidence is hidden, and never overwrite it with later messages, a final, an exception or expiry. An accepted well-formed unavailable receipt or first global receipt-revocation message is not a parent rejection; retain its existing flag without inventing malformed transport.

Closed first-site catalogue:

```text
budget-before-launch
message-envelope | message-after-final | message-count
source-duplicate | source-invalid | source-required
delta-envelope-or-time
frame-invalid | progress-replay | costs-replay | workflow-replay
receipt-after-revocation
p09-duplicate | p09-invalid | p11-duplicate | p11-invalid | p12-duplicate | p12-invalid
receipt-revocation-envelope-or-duplicate
exit-envelope-or-duplicate | exit-invalid-or-time
final-envelope | final-sanitizer | final-binding
message-role | ipc-byte-limit | message-handler-exception
controller-send-exception | watchdog-exception
```

One code may intentionally identify an existing compound guard rather than its individual predicate. Never rerun a validator, projection, JSON comparison, source check or clock to manufacture a finer explanation. For the three replay guards, a private constant stage set immediately before the original call can identify the first false result. Original predicates, evaluation order, short circuiting, invocation counts, thrown identities, rejection/clearing and returned report remain unchanged. A failed observer cannot make a failing check pass or interrupt the original guard. Observation overhead stays inside existing budgets.

The first-rejection record has only these fields: `site`, `role`, `counted_messages`, `accounted_ipc_bytes`, `validated_frame_sequence`, `frame_sequence`, `source_seen`, `source_valid`, `final_seen`, `final_valid`, `receipt_set_revoked`. Role is an allowlisted alias (`source`, `frame`, `p09`, `p11`, `p12`, `receipts-unavailable`, `exit`, `final`, `other`) or null; never copy an unknown role string. `frame_sequence` is copied only from the already sanitized current frame, otherwise null. `validated_frame_sequence` is the last frame that passed all three projections, before any later aggregate-byte rejection; it is not a trusted-output or liveness claim. No extra snapshot call is permitted.

Use existing counted-message/accounted-byte values, not a newly claimed receive ordinal: their increments are short-circuited by original guards. Bounds are counted messages 0..50009, frame sequences 0..50000 and accounted bytes 0..(B + 131072), where unchanged B = 8388608 + 2 * 131072 + 50000 * 128. The last bound covers a single original final-message increment before the unchanged byte guard rejects it; it does not enlarge that guard. Flags describe existing private state only. Retain no source/fixture path, hash dictionary, module/case name, PID, task/proof/context, payload, output/error body, stack, token, credential or arbitrary object in this record.

Terminal branch catalogue follows the original return checks: `budget-before-launch`, `revoked`, `controller-not-closed`, `controller-expired`, `controller-supervision-failed`, `controller-exit-nonzero`, `final-missing`, `return-budget`, `watchdog-exception`, `report-returned`. Preserve the original left-to-right terminal OR evaluation, including how often `deadline.remaining()` is called. Do not convert controller/child signals or elapsed time into another branch. The terminal slot has exactly `branch`, `original_status` (`completed`, `failed`, `deadline`) and `controller_supervision`. The last is null unless the original await already obtained its tuple; otherwise copy only `exit_code`, `closed`, `expired`, `failed`. A nullable safe-integer exit code retains its sign, including a negative controller code, without normalization or reuse of the different child-exit sanitizer; unsupported tuple data makes only the diagnosis unavailable. This tuple belongs to the issued controller fork, not the Vitest child's `native_exit`/`stdio_close`, and never proves descendant quiescence or cleanup.

Seal the claimed handle at its original return boundary before creating a detached closed schema-1 `p12-observation-loss` receipt. Fixed fields: `observation_point: watchdog-return`, `diagnostic_only: true`, `acceptance: false`, `sealed: true`, `capture_status`, `unavailable_reason`, `first_rejection`, `terminal`, plus `schema_version` and `kind`. Captured receipts have null unavailable reason, nullable first rejection and a known terminal slot; null first rejection means only no parent rejection was observed. Unavailable receipts use a fixed reason (`invalid-binding`, `incomplete-observation`, `invalid-state`) and null measurement slots. After sealing, diagnostic notifications are inert before any additional clock/state/payload access; original handler behavior remains unchanged. The diagnosis never repairs the original P12 report or grants acceptance.

#### Bounded output without new transport or a larger cap

The same CLI P12 branch may emit one best-effort `P12_OBSERVATION_LOSS_PROFILE ` JSON line on its own stderr, after the original stdout report write and exit-code assignment. The existing P12 stdout JSON/status and all other workloads' output remain unchanged. No child output is forwarded. The diagnostic line is at most **2048 whole UTF-8 bytes including prefix/LF**, with at most one native write, no retry, drain wait, callback-driven action or file logging. Catch only the additional diagnostic write failure; retain the original stdout/exit behavior and its existing exception path.

Preserve the 131072-byte whole-final ceiling by checking the exact joint serialized size of the existing P12 role-wrapped final (plus its LF) and diagnostic line. Use the already serialized original report bytes and constant wrapper overhead; do not reserialize private messages or reevaluate validators. If the extra line cannot fit, suppress only that additional line, without truncating/dropping any original evidence or changing exit/status/caps. Missing/suppressed/malformed diagnosis is unknown, never evidence that no rejection occurred. Prove the normal and maximum real joint envelopes and the suppression edge in synthetic tests. The diagnostic itself stays below 2048 bytes; original stdout/stderr, progress/frame, case/module/tail, final/IPC byte and 50008-message ceilings remain intact. P13 sends **zero** additional IPC messages.

### T015-P14 proposed delta-condition design - 2026-10-15 local

**Current authority: design only.** The owner authorized a narrow design to distinguish which condition failed inside `delta-envelope-or-time`, with unchanged guards/timeouts and no new campaign. Only this design and the existing task plan are writable now. This is a transient diagnostic delta within T015, not a new persisted product entity, workflow, runtime or acceptance authority. The failed P12 campaign, captured P13 receipt and unknown suite/Planner/cleanup facts remain unchanged historical evidence. T015/T016 stay unchecked.

#### Source-verified scope and four conditions

The existing parent watchdog first admits a plain bounded message, requires source metadata and identifies one of four delta roles. Its next guard is exactly:

```ts
if (!suiteExact(value, keys) || !spanTime(data.received_ms) || data.received_ms < lastDelivery || data.received_ms > deadline.elapsed()) {
  reject("delta-envelope-or-time", data); revoked = true; return;
}
```

`suiteExact` checks the already selected three-field envelope; it must not be broadened. `spanTime` admits only finite numeric offsets in 0..900000 ms. The strict comparisons are intentional current behavior: preserve `<` and `>`, equality behavior, evaluation order, short circuiting and the one existing parent clock call only when its operand is reached. The last campaign proves only the compound failure, not any particular row below; no historical condition or timestamp can be backfilled.

| New closed condition code | Existing predicate that rejected | Already consumed numeric context permitted in the new receipt |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `envelope-shape` | `!suiteExact(value, keys)` | None; all time fields null |
| `received-ms-range` | `!spanTime(data.received_ms)` | None; do not retain the invalid value, its type, properties or error |
| `delivery-regression` | `data.received_ms < lastDelivery` | Validated received offset and the previous-delivery operand; parent offset null |
| `after-parent-elapsed` | `data.received_ms > deadline.elapsed()` | Validated received offset, previous-delivery operand and the return value of that original clock call |

Tag the original OR evaluation with a private constant stage, reset per delta. Advance the tag immediately before each original operand only when earlier operands passed. Do not split/replace the admission policy, wrap predicates in new callbacks or rerun a validator to select a reason. Copy `lastDelivery` only while evaluating its existing operand, without changing that variable. Reuse the return of the original `deadline.elapsed()` call; do not obtain a second sample. Copy the received offset only after the original `spanTime` passed, from its existing private plain record. For the first two rows, do not inspect that value further. No new clock, snapshot, source/state read, timer, listener, retry or arithmetic tolerance is permitted. Observation overhead remains inside the original deadline.

Capture a condition only when the original guard actually evaluates true and that guard is the first P13 rejection. Accepted deltas discard their temporary tags/operands. If an original predicate/clock throws, preserve the existing `message-handler-exception` path and thrown identity; do not call it a false predicate or invent a P14 condition. Preserve `lastDelivery = data.received_ms` only after the original guard passes, and leave frame sanitization, all three projections, receipts, byte/message accounting, cancellation, terminal OR, return object/status and cleanup untouched. No rejected evidence is restored. A new observer failure cannot interrupt the guard, affect P13 or make any failing check pass.

#### Closed minimal P14 receipt and privacy

The new schema-1 side receipt has exactly `schema_version: 1`, `kind: p14-delta-rejection`, `observation_point: watchdog-return`, `diagnostic_only: true`, `acceptance: false`, `sealed: true`, `capture_status: captured | unavailable`, `unavailable_reason` and `delta`. Captured receipts have null unavailable reason and one closed `delta` object with exactly:

```text
condition: envelope-shape | received-ms-range | delivery-regression | after-parent-elapsed
role: frame | p09 | p11 | p12
counted_messages: integer 0..50009, matching the first P13 rejection
received_ms: finite number 0..900000 or null
previous_delivery_ms: finite number 0..900000 or null
parent_elapsed_ms: finite number 0..900000 or null
```

Nullability is determined by the table, never by extra measurement. Keep fractional numeric operands without rounding, clamping, epsilon, subtraction-derived categories or clock normalization. The separate closed sanitizer may check primitive bounds and required/null fields, without calling original message validators or recomputing comparisons to classify/repair a condition. Unsupported operand state makes only P14 unavailable. Unavailable receipts have `delta: null` and one fixed reason: `invalid-binding`, `incomplete-observation`, `invalid-state` or `not-target-rejection`. The last means no captured first target rejection, not that the original workload passed. An unavailable P13 binding remains unavailable in P14, not a fallback cause.

Reader precedence is explicit: before its owned return seal use `incomplete-observation`; after sealing, a different report reference gets `invalid-binding`; invalid/unavailable private P13 evidence or invalid P14 state gets `invalid-state`; valid P13 evidence with no first target gets `not-target-rejection`. A valid target requires a present matching candidate; missing/mismatched target data is `invalid-state`, never inferred from final status or replayed source. No unavailable receipt carries partial numeric data.

Store no raw message/value, invalid timestamp type/value, frame contents/sequence, source/hash dictionary, module/case/task/proof/context, path, PID, exception/stack, credential, token, arbitrary property or child output. These scalar times are only operands of one failed parent admission comparison. They do not prove clock synchronization, scheduler latency, an inner Planner failure, full-suite timing or cleanup. P13's 458/453 historical counters are not test counts; the old outer-controller closure remains distinct from missing Vitest closure.

Accept only exact occurrences of this retained case's own canonical test-file path (including the matching local file-URL form), then export only line 1..10000 / column 1..65536. Discard the path/function/text; the enclosing case source is the sole site source. Do not export other repository or external paths. Optional JSON declaration location uses the same scalar bounds. Mark locations `reported-not-source-verified`, `unavailable`, or, for stack-site truncation only, `limit-reached`; no source-line reads, arbitrary path decoding/traversal or coordinate repair. Unsupported text/locations remain unavailable, not reconstructed from source guesses.

The fixed error representation plus source/ordinal/location is intended to locate the failing assertion and distinguish timeout/assertion/typed failures safely. It is **not verbatim error capture**, and an unsupported classification can still require a later focused owner decision. No message from the previous run is recoverable by this design. Classification completeness is separate from identity capture and cannot prove a cause, successful suite or cleanup.

#### Closed P15 contracts and binding

Define two strict local DTO variants, no persisted JSON Schema: controller candidate uses `observation_point: controller-final-json`; parent receipt uses `observation_point: watchdog-return`. Both have exactly `schema_version: 1`, `kind: p15-suite-failures`, `diagnostic_only: true`, `acceptance: false`, `sealed: true`, `capture_status`, `unavailable_reason`, `binding`, `failed_total`, `retained_cases`, `omitted_cases`, `classification_complete`, `cases`, plus their fixed observation point. Reject unknown keys/enums and hostile/mutable input; return detached data.

- Captured binding has exactly `source_fingerprint`, `progress_sequence`, `module_count`, `passed_tests`, `failed_tests`, `skipped_tests`. Fingerprint is SHA-256 of the fixed twenty-path ordered `[path, existing hash]` pairs; it adds no reads or source inventory. Sequence is 1..50000, modules 1..256 and totals 0..20000, with the existing sum constraint. Source fingerprint is consistency evidence, not authentication or a hash of every test file.
- Captured `failed_total` is 1..20000, equals binding failed count, retained cases is `min(2, failed_total)`, omitted cases is the exact difference. Cases have exactly `module: { ordinal, source }`, `case_ordinal`, nullable `full_name_sha256`, nullable `declaration: { line, column }`, `declaration_status`, `errors`. Sorted unique tuples must match the private selection. Null declaration requires `unavailable`; a present declaration requires `reported-not-source-verified`.
- Each errors object has exactly `capture_status`, `unavailable_reason`, `reported_count`, `retained_count`, `omitted_count`, `classification_complete`, `entries`. Captured errors have null reason, count 1..20000, retained `min(2,count)` and exact omitted difference. Entries have exactly `index` (0 or 1 in original order), `reported_name`, `signature`, canonical `message`, `sites`, `positions_status`; each site has exactly line/column with the preceding bounds. Sanitize masks/signature/message/name relationships, not arbitrary text. Missing/invalid errors use `missing-errors` or `invalid-errors`, null reported/omitted counts, zero retained, empty entries and false completeness. Case identity remains retained.
- Root completeness is true only when no cases/errors were omitted and every retained case has captured, fully classified errors; it is never an acceptance field. Positions/name-digest availability is reported separately. Unavailable root reasons are closed: `incomplete-observation`, `invalid-binding`, `not-target-report`, `missing-candidate`, `invalid-transport`, `invalid-json`, `invalid-state`. Root unavailable has null binding/failed/omitted fields, zero retained, false completeness and empty cases; no partial numeric or private data. Controller-side preparation failures use only invalid-json/invalid-state; they do not replace original results.

In the parent, extend only the existing P13 factory-owned one-use handle with independent P15 state and detached `readFailures(report)`. Preserve existing claim, exact report reference, seal, P13/P14 readers and invalid flags. While the existing frame handler processes a `case-result` that has passed **all three original projections**, a separately caught P15 observer maintains only failed count and the lowest two validated failure tuples. No replay/snapshot/clock/IO call is added. Check the original owned/unsealed state before new frame access; late/foreign/reused/reentrant observation is inert. A P15 fault never revokes progress or sets P13/P14 invalid/first slots.

At the original return seal, capture private binding facts before caller mutation, using only already admitted report/source/summary data and the P15 failed-frame selection. Reader precedence: before seal incomplete; wrong exact report reference invalid-binding; invalid P13 evidence invalid-state; anything except P13 `first_rejection: null`, `report-returned` and a complete failed report with positive JSON failed count is not-target-report. Then distinguish missing candidate from invalid transport, propagate a valid unavailable candidate's fixed reason, or require exact fingerprint/sequence/module/count and selected-tuple equality before captured publication. Native exit/stdio-close/source facts stay independently bound to the admitted report; a nonzero Vitest exit is allowed failure evidence, never turned into success. Reads must not inspect caller report contents. A failed-suite file/hook error with no failed assertions stays outside this narrowly defined target.

#### Separate existing stderr lane, zero new IPC

The controller candidate cannot live only in a WeakMap: the current parent is another process. Do not add an IPC role/message, extend the final envelope, change `++messages > 50008`, or forward child errors as execution data. Reuse the controller's existing native stderr pipe and the parent's existing single `controller.stderr.on("data", ...)` drain: replace only its no-op body with a bounded P15 collector when an owned capture exists. Keep the stdout drain and supervisor listeners/order unchanged. Other callers still drain/discard. Construct collector state lazily without clock/FS/dependency reads; no new timer, listener, process, file or logger.

After the original final `process.send` returns normally, the controller may attempt one `P15_SUITE_FAILURES_CANDIDATE ` JSON/LF native stderr write, <=4096 whole UTF-8 bytes, in its own catch. Keep the original send arguments, callback, disconnect and exit assignment unchanged. The candidate belongs to the exact returned report from that invocation; a false write/send return is not delivery proof. No retry, drain wait, callback action, raw output or additional IPC. Controller preparation/format/write failure can only lose P15 evidence.

The parent collector retains at most one <=4096-byte reserved line, drains but never exports unrelated stderr, and has an independent cumulative 8 MiB input ceiling using the existing constant. Malformed/duplicate/truncated reserved lines, duplicate/escaped keys, invalid UTF-8 or ceiling overflow permanently invalidate only P15; later plausible data cannot heal it. Memory remains bounded even for long unrelated lines. Use exact internal marker/DTO variant, never the public marker as an alias. Final IPC and stderr may arrive in either order; freeze at the original supervised controller close/return with no extra wait. Missing/late data remains missing. These new side-evidence validation bounds do not modify original execution guards or process supervision.

Keep the CLI workload selection, original stdout serialization/write, exit assignment and single `observation.emit(report, line)` unchanged. The helper still attempts original P13 first and original target-only P14 next. Only after the normal P13 write return may it attempt one `P15_SUITE_FAILURES_PROFILE ` line for a target failed report, containing captured or explicitly unavailable P15 data. Capture/public emission is once-only in the same latch, independent catch, no raw messages or report reserialization. If P13 was suppressed/threw, suppress P15 too. A P14 target implies revoked/non-target P15 evidence; explicitly enforce that any P14 attempt excludes P15, preserving **at most two public diagnostic stderr writes** per invocation. P13/P14 each stay <=2048; P15 alone is <=4096. P15 cannot replace old evidence.

Using already serialized strings and constant wrapper overhead, enforce exact **role-wrapped original final/LF + P13 + any emitted P14/P15 <=131072 bytes**. If P15 does not fit, suppress only P15, without trimming/dropping/reordering old data or raising a cap. Controller candidate is also <=4096, independently validated; no IPC bytes/messages are added. A static ASCII width-superset model with maximum path/digest/counter widths, two cases/two errors/two sites and 80-byte canonical-message slots is **2923 bytes including internal prefix/LF**. This independent-field width superset deliberately includes counter combinations/duplicate tuples disallowed by semantic validation; it is design arithmetic, not a valid workload sample or proof of implemented serialization/joint-envelope tests. Runtime whole-line checks and maximum real joint tests remain required. Missing output, asynchronous I/O failure or lack of flush confirmation remains unknown; no listener is added to pursue it.

Preserve the original 900000/30000 ms limits, two workers, all case/hook/HTTP/runtime deadlines, TERM/KILL behavior, 8 MiB original streams, 2048-byte progress frames, 50000 frames, 20000 cases/64 open, 256 modules, tail32, original final/IPC aggregate bounds, source checks, cleanup and acceptance policy. No failing test is fixed, filtered, retried, skipped, renamed or given a longer timeout by P15.

#### P16-A: partial identity independent of optional detail

Add independent factory-owned P16 state/read/seal/formatting to the existing P13 handle. It must not mutate P13/P14/P15 flags, receipts, target decisions or selections. An invalid P15 identity latch cannot be treated as valid empty evidence.

Eligibility is deliberately different from P15: P13 captured, first rejection null, `report-returned`, original `failed` or `deadline`, null final JSON, available admitted progress with at least one observed module, a bounded nonnegative observed failed count, matching private count/selection and unchanged complete twenty-source before/after dictionaries. Zero observed failures is permitted only as a proven partial counter, with zero retained entries and false error-classification completeness, never a whole-suite no-error claim; reported timing may still be useful. A completed failed-JSON report continues through unchanged P15; a rejected/unbound original report remains unavailable. P16 never changes original status, exit, JSON, progress, native supervision, cleanup or acceptance. Integers use the existing 50000-frame/256-module/20000-case bounds and original count arithmetic.

The failures block names `lowest-two-admitted-tuples`, observed/retained/omitted counts and at most two sorted unique entries with only relative module source/ordinal, case ordinal and the existing P15 normalized errors block. Retained is `min(2, observed failures)` and omitted is the exact difference. Error classification completeness describes only observed retained errors, never a complete suite or acceptance. Locations, when present in normalized errors, remain own-file reported/unverified scalar coordinates.
